\displaystyle\mathcal{C}=\{x_{l}+iv:v\in[0,v_{0}]\}\cup\mathcal{C}_{u}\cup\{x_{r}+iv:v\in[0,v_{0}]\}

such that the closed curve
\mathcal{C}\cup\bar{\mathcal{C}}
is contained in a region where
f
is analytic. Further, for some null sequence
(\varepsilon_{n})
satisfying

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{n}\geq m^{-\alpha}

(D.16)

for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
, we introduce

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{l,n}

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}\{x_{l}+iv:~v\in[m^{-1}\varepsilon_{n},v_{0}]\},&\text{if }x_{l}>0\\
\{x_{l}+iv:~v\in[0,v_{0}]\}&\text{if }x_{l}\leq 0,\end{cases}

(D.17)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{r,n}

\displaystyle:=\{x_{r}+iv:~v\in[m^{-1}\varepsilon_{n},v_{0}]\}

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{n}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{C}_{l,n}\cup\mathcal{C}_{u}\cup\mathcal{C}_{r,n}.

(D.18)

Lastly, we define

\displaystyle\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z)=\begin{cases}M_{n}^{*}(z),&\text{for }z\in\mathcal{C}_{n},\\
M_{n}^{*}(x_{r}+im^{-1}\varepsilon_{n}),&\text{for }z=x_{r}+iv\text{ with }v\in[0,m^{-1}\varepsilon_{n}]\\
M_{n}^{*}(x_{l}+im^{-1}\varepsilon_{n}),&\text{for }z=x_{l}+iv\text{ with }x_{l}>0\text{ and }v\in[0,m^{-1}\varepsilon_{n}],\end{cases}

and because of
m(\bar{z})=\overline{m(z)}
for any Stieltjes transform
m
, we have

\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(\bar{z}):=\overline{\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z)}
for
z\in\mathcal{C}
.
Since

\displaystyle\widehat{T}_{n}^{*}(f)\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}

\displaystyle-q\int fd\mu_{p/n,{\mu^{\Sigma_{n}}}}^{0}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ointctrclockwise_{\mathcal{C}\cup\bar{\mathcal{C}}}f(z)M_{n}^{*}(z)\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}\dd z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ointctrclockwise_{\mathcal{C}\cup\bar{\mathcal{C}}}f(z)\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z)\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}\dd z+\mathcal{O}\bigg(8\varepsilon_{n}{q\over m}\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}\cup\bar{\mathcal{C}}}\Big(\big|K_{\rm right}-x_{r}\big|^{-1}+\big|K_{\rm left}-x_{l}\big|^{-1}\Big)\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ointctrclockwise_{\mathcal{C}\cup\bar{\mathcal{C}}}f(z)\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z)\dd z+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

(D.19)

by Corollary
4.5
, is sufficient to consider
\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}
in what follows.
The essential part of the proof of Theorem
4.6
consists of verifying the following Donsker-type result. Gaussianity of (
D.15
)
then follows with (
D.19
) from the continuous mapping Theorem.

Proposition D.3
(Functional CLT for the conditional process
\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}
in probability).

Grant the conditions of Theorem
4.6
, then

\displaystyle d_{BL}\bigg\{\mathcal{L}\Big(\big(\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z)-\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z)\big]\big)_{z\in\mathcal{C}}\Big\arrowvert X_{1},\dots,X_{n},\Pi_{n}\Big),\,\mathcal{L}(Z)\bigg\}\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}0

with a centered Gaussian process
(Z)
on
\mathcal{C}
satisfying
Z(\bar{z})=\overline{Z(z)}
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big(Z(z_{1}),Z(z_{2})\big)=2\frac{(\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0})^{\prime}(z_{1})(\underline{m}_{c,{H}}^{0})^{\prime}(z_{2})}{\big(\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0}(z_{1})-\underline{m}_{c,{H}}^{0}(z_{2})\big)^{2}}-\frac{2}{(z_{1}-z_{2})^{2}}\ \text{ for }z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathcal{C}

(understood as its continuous extrapolation for the removable singularities at
z_{1}=z_{2}
).

Proposition D.4
.

Grant the conditions of Theorem
4.6
, then

\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\bigg|\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z)\big]-c\int\frac{(\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0}(z))^{3}t^{2}\,d{H}(t)}{(1+t\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0}(z))^{3}}\Big[1-c\int\frac{(\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0}(z))^{2}t^{2}\,d{H}(t)}{(1+t\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0}(z))^{2}}\Big]^{-2}\bigg|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

A central tool in the proof of this result is an
analog of Proposition
B.1
for the truncation in Section
D.1
with a bound which is not depending on
z\in\mathcal{C}_{n}
.
These results will be presented first in the following section. The proof of Proposition
D.3

is given in Section
E.4
, while Proposition
D.4
is proved in Section
E.4
.

To conclude the proof, we note that it follows (
D.15
) that

\displaystyle\hat{T}_{n}^{*}(f)-q\int fd\mu_{p/n,{\mu^{\Sigma_{n}}}}^{0}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\ointctrclockwise f(z)\big(\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z)-\mathbb{E}^{*}[\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z)]\big)\dd z

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\ointctrclockwise f(z)\mathbb{E}^{*}[\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z)]\dd z+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Therefore, by Proposition
D.3
and
D.4
and the continuous mapping theorem,

d_{BL}\bigg(\mathcal{L}\bigg(\hat{T}_{n}^{*}(f)-q\int fd\mu_{p/n,{\mu^{\Sigma_{n}}}}^{0}~\Big|Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{n}\bigg)~,~{\cal N}(\mu,\sigma^{2})\bigg)=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

where
\mu
and
\sigma^{2}
are expectation and variance of the limiting normal distribution of the statistic

\hat{T}_{n}(f)-p\int fd\mu_{p/n,{\mu^{\Sigma_{n}}}}^{0}

(
Bai and Silverstein,, 1998
, see)
. Finally, we note that

\displaystyle{m\over n}\hat{T}_{n}(f)-q\int fd\mu_{p/n,{\mu^{\Sigma_{n}}}}^{0}={m\over n}\Big(\hat{T}_{n}(f)-p\int fd\mu_{p/n,{\mu^{\Sigma_{n}}}}^{0}\Big)=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

which completes the proof of Theorem
4.6
.

E
Proofs of Propositions
D.3
and
D.4

E.1
Non-standard results on quadratic forms

For the statement of an
analog of Proposition
B.1
for the truncation in Section
D.1
we study the following matrices in the quadratic form

\displaystyle C^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C^{*}(z)=L_{n}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}L_{n},

(E.1)

\displaystyle C^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C^{*}(z)=L_{n}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}ML_{n},

(E.2)

\displaystyle C^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C^{*}(z)=L_{n}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-2}L_{n},

(E.3)

\displaystyle C^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})=L_{n}^{\top}D_{12}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{12}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}~,

(E.4)

\displaystyle C^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})=L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big[D_{1j}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big]L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{1j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}~,

(E.5)

\displaystyle C^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C^{*}(z)=L_{n}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-2}L_{n},

(E.6)

\displaystyle C^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})=L_{n}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z_{1})^{-2}D_{1}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n},

(E.7)

\displaystyle C^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})=L_{n}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}D_{1}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}

(E.8)

for
z,z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{n}
.
M\in\mathbb{C}^{q\times q}
is deterministic and of bounded spectral norm, uniformly in
n
. Recall that the notation
\mathbb{E}_{X}
means integration with respect to
X=(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})
. In other words (as the projection is independent of
X
), the expectation is taken conditional on
\Pi_{n}
.

Proposition E.1
.

For any

p\geq 2
, there exists some constant
K_{p}>0
, such that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X}\big\arrowvert X_{1}^{*\top}C^{*}X_{1}^{*}-\tr C^{*}\big\arrowvert^{p}\leq K_{p}\Big(m^{p-1}\delta_{m}^{2p-4}+\frac{m^{p+1}}{n}\Big)~,

where
K_{p}
is a constant depending
only on
p
and the matrix

C^{*}
is given by one of the matrices in (
E.3
) - (
E.8
).

We
denote by

\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,1,\ldots,j}^{*}
the matrix which is obtained from
\widehat{\Sigma}_{n}^{*}
by omitting the terms involving
X_{1}^{*},\ldots,X_{j}^{*}

(for
j=0
this is
\Sigma_{n}^{*}
) and the matrix

\tilde{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{*}

is the empirical covariance matrix of the vectors
\tilde{X}_{2}^{*},\dots,\tilde{X}_{m}^{*}
with normalizing factor
1/m
which are defined in (
B.9
). By Corollary
4.5
(with an adaptation of its proof to the matrices
\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{*},\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,1,2}^{*}

we find constants
K_{\rm left}
and
K_{\rm right}

such that
the event

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{n}:=\Big\{

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}(\tilde{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{*})\geq K_{\rm left},~\|\tilde{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{*}\|_{S_{\infty}}\leq K_{\rm right},~

(E.9)

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,1,\ldots,j}^{*})\geq K_{\rm left},~\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,1,\ldots,j}^{*}\|_{S_{\infty}}\leq K_{\rm right}~\text{for }j=0,1,2\Big\},

satisfies for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{n}^{c})=o(m^{-\ell})~.

(E.10)

We will begin proving the statement for matrices of the form (
E.1
). Observing the arguments as given in the proof of Proposition
B.1
we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X}\big\arrowvert X_{1}^{*\top}C^{*}X_{1}^{*}-\tr C^{*}\big\arrowvert^{p}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-1}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{X}\big\arrowvert X_{1}^{\top}\tilde{C}^{*}X_{1}-\tr\tilde{C}^{*}\big\arrowvert^{p}

(E.11)

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~+\mathbb{E}_{X}\big\arrowvert X_{1}^{\top}(\tilde{C}^{*}-C^{*})X_{1}-\tr(\tilde{C}^{*}-C^{*})\big\arrowvert^{p}\Big)~,

where matrix
\tilde{C}^{*}
is defined by (
B.19
) with
M=I_{q}
.
By the same reasoning leading to equation (3.2) in
Bai and Silverstein, (2004)
with
a(v)=1
and
B(v)=\tilde{C}^{*}
it follows that

\mathbb{E}_{X}\big\arrowvert X_{1}^{\top}\tilde{C}^{*}X_{1}-\tr\tilde{C}^{*}\big\arrowvert^{p}\leq c{\delta_{m}^{2p-4\vee 0}\over m^{1\wedge p}}=c{\delta_{m}^{2p-4}\over m}~.

Note that the matrix
\tilde{C}^{*}
satisfies the corresponding assumption for such an estimate, that is

\displaystyle\|\tilde{C}^{*}\|_{S_{\infty}}\leq\|L_{n}\|_{S_{\infty}}^{2}\|(\tilde{D}^{*}_{1}(z))^{-1}\|_{S_{\infty}}\leq c(\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}+\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}^{c}}m^{1+\alpha})\leq c(1+\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}^{c}}m^{1+\alpha}),

(E.12)

where
\tilde{D}_{1}^{*}(z)
is defined as
D_{1}^{*}(z)
with
X_{2}^{*},\dots,X_{m}^{*}
replaced by
\tilde{X}_{2}^{*},\dots,\tilde{X}_{m}^{*}
.

Now we turn to the second term in (
E.11
) and consider

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X}\big|\tr(\tilde{C}^{*}-C^{*})\big|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq m^{p}\mathbb{E}_{X}\big\|\tilde{C}^{*}-C^{*}\big\|_{S_{\infty}}^{p}\leq m^{p}\|L_{n}\|_{S_{\infty}}^{2p}\mathbb{E}_{X}\big\|D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}-\tilde{D}_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big\|_{S_{\infty}}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq cm^{p}\mathbb{E}_{X}\big[\|D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\|_{S_{\infty}}^{p}\|\tilde{D}_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\|_{S_{\infty}}^{p}\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{*}-\tilde{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{*}\|_{S_{\infty}}^{p}\big],

where the last inequality follows from the formula
B^{-1}-A^{-1}=A^{-1}(A-B)B^{-1}
.
Note that we have

\displaystyle\|D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\|_{S_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i=1}^{q}{1\over|z-\lambda_{i}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{*})|}\leq\max_{i=1}^{q}{1\over|\Re(z-\lambda_{i}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{*}))|}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}+\frac{\arrowvert z\arrowvert}{\Im(z)}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}^{c}}

(E.13)

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big\{{1\over|x_{r}-K_{\rm right}|},{1\over|x_{l}-K_{\rm left}|}\Big\}+\frac{\arrowvert z\arrowvert}{\Im(z)}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}^{c}}

where
K_{\rm left}
and
K_{\rm right}
are the constants from Corollary
4.5
. Moreover,

\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{*}-\tilde{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{*}\|_{S_{\infty}}\leq\|\Sigma_{n}\|_{S_{\infty}}\|X_{1}\|^{2}{\Delta_{n}^{*}\over m},

and

\displaystyle{1\over m^{p/2}}\mathbb{E}_{X}\|X_{1}\|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq c,

(E.14)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X}\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq c_{p}{m\over n}

(E.15)

(note that (
E.14
) follows from the fact that here the random variable
X_{1}
is of dimension
q^{\prime}
and has independent components bounded by
\delta_{m}\sqrt{m}
).
Combining these estimates and
and using the corresponding bound for the quantity
\|\tilde{D}_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\|_{S_{\infty}}

and obtain (observing (
E.10
)), we arrive at

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X}\big|

\displaystyle\tr(\tilde{C}^{*}-C^{*})\big|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq cm^{p}\Big\{\mathbb{E}_{X}\big[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{*}-\tilde{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{*}\|_{S_{\infty}}^{p}\big]+\big|{z\over\Im(z)}\big|^{2p}\mathbb{E}_{X}\big[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}^{c}}\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{*}-\tilde{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{*}\|_{S_{\infty}}^{p}\big]\big\}

\displaystyle\leq cm^{p}\Big\{{1\over m^{p}}\mathbb{E}_{X}|\Delta_{n}^{*}|^{p}\mathbb{E}_{X}\|X_{1}\|^{2p}+\big|{z\over\Im(z)}\big|^{2p}\big(\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{A}_{n}^{c}})\big)^{1/2}\big(\mathbb{E}_{X}\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{*}-\tilde{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{*}\|_{S_{\infty}}^{2p}\big)^{1/2}\big\}

\displaystyle\leq cm^{p}\Big\{{m\over n}+m^{2p(1+\alpha)}(\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{A}_{n}^{c}})\big)^{1/2}\sqrt{m\over n}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq c{m^{p+1}\over n}~.

(E.16)

Similarly, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X}\big\|X_{1}^{\top}(\tilde{C}^{*}-C^{*})X_{1}\big|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{X}\big[\|X_{1}\|1^{2p}\|C^{*}-C^{*}\|^{p}_{S_{\infty}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq c\mathbb{E}_{X}\Big[\|X_{1}\|^{2p}\|D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\|_{S_{\infty}}^{p}\|\tilde{D}_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\|_{S_{\infty}}^{p}\|X_{1}\|^{2p}\big({\Delta_{n}^{*}\over m}\big)^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq{c\over m^{p}}\Big\{\mathbb{E}_{X}\big[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}|\Delta_{n}^{*}|^{p}\|X_{1}\|^{4p}\big]+\big|{z\over\Im(z)}\big|^{2p}\mathbb{E}_{X}\big[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}^{c}}|\Delta_{n}^{*}|^{p}\|X_{1}\|^{4p}\big]\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq{c\over m^{p}}\Big\{{m^{2p+1}\over n}+m^{(1+\alpha)2p}\big(\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{A}_{n}^{c}})\big)^{1/2}\big(\mathbb{E}_{X}[|\Delta_{n}^{*}|^{2p}\|X_{1}\|^{8p}]\big)^{1/2}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq c{m^{p+1}\over n},

by (
E.10
). Combining this estimate with (
E.16
) and (
E.12
) yields the statement of Proposition
E.1
for the matrix (
E.1
).

The statement for the other matrices follow by similar arguments, which are omitted for the sake of brevity. For, example, for the matrix (
E.6
) we use the identities

\displaystyle\tilde{D}_{1}^{*}(z)^{-2}-D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-2}

\displaystyle=D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-2}\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{2}-\tilde{D}_{1}^{*}(z)^{2}\big)\tilde{D}_{1}^{*}(z)^{-2}

\displaystyle D_{1}^{*}(z)^{2}-\tilde{D}_{1}^{*}(z)^{2}

\displaystyle=\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)-\tilde{D}_{1}^{*}(z)\big)\tilde{D}_{1}^{*}(z)+D_{1}^{*}(z)\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)-\tilde{D}_{1}^{*}(z)\big)

which gives

\displaystyle\|\tilde{D}_{1}^{*}(z)^{-2}-D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-2}\|_{S_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c\|\tilde{D}_{1}^{*}(z)^{-2}\|_{S_{\infty}}\|D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-2}\|_{S_{\infty}}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\times\big(2|z|+\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{*}\|_{S_{\infty}}+\|\tilde{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{*}\|_{S_{\infty}}\big)\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{*}-\tilde{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{*}\|_{S_{\infty}}.

We now proceed in the same way as before
multiplying with
(\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}+\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}^{c})
, where we use

\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{*}\|^{2}_{S_{\infty}}\leq\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{*}\|^{2}_{S_{2}}\leq{1\over m^{2}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\|X_{i}\|^{2}\|X_{j}\|^{2}

on
\mathcal{A}_{n}^{c}
.
∎

Remark E.2
.

Note that Proposition
E.1
will replace Proposition
B.1
in the following discussion. Moreover, in the case
p=2
both results yield the same estimate. This fact will be of importance as we will use some of the
estimates for Section
B.4
-
B.6
in the following discussion which also hold under Assumption (A3+) (instead of (A3)) and the truncation scheme considered in this section.

Proposition E.3
.

\displaystyle\sup_{z_{1},z_{2}\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big\arrowvert X_{1}^{*\top}C^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})X_{1}^{*}-\tr C^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})\big\arrowvert^{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(m+\frac{m^{3}}{n}\Big)

\displaystyle\sup_{z_{1},z_{2}\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big\arrowvert X_{1}^{*\top}C^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})X_{1}^{*}-\tr C^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})\big\arrowvert^{4}=O_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(m^{3}\delta_{n}^{4}+\frac{m^{5}}{n}\Big)~,

where

\displaystyle C^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=(D_{1}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-1}

\displaystyle C^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=(D_{12}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-1}

\displaystyle C^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z_{1})\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}

\displaystyle C^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=(D_{1}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-1}(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z_{1})\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}(D_{1}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-1}

\displaystyle C^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=C^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})=L_{n}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z_{1})^{-2}D_{1}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n},

\displaystyle C^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=C^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})=L_{n}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}D_{1}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n},

Exemplary, we consider a matrix with
z=z_{1}=z_{2}
, for which we use the notation
C^{*}(z):=C^{*}(z,z)
(the other cases can be treated similarly).
Let
\hat{I}=\{i_{1}^{*},\ldots,i_{m}^{*}\}
denote the random subset of chosen indices by the bootstrap and note that
\#\hat{I}\leq m
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big\arrowvert X_{1}^{*\top}C^{*}(z)X_{1}^{*}-\tr C^{*}(z)\big\arrowvert^{4}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[{1\over n}\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big|X_{i}^{\top}C^{*}(z)X_{i}-{\rm tr}(C^{*}(z))\big|^{4}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}{1\over n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}}\big|X_{i}^{\top}C^{*}(z)X_{i}-{\rm tr}(C^{*}(z))\big|^{4}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}{1\over n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}^{c}}\big|X_{i}^{\top}C^{*}(z)X_{i}-{\rm tr}(C^{*}(z))\big|^{4}\bigg]

\displaystyle=:\mathbb{E}[S_{1}]+\mathbb{E}[S_{2}]~.

(E.17)

We will now consider both terms separately starting with
\mathbb{E}[S_{1}]
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[S_{1}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}{1\over n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}}\big|X_{i}^{\top}C^{*}(z)X_{i}-{\rm tr}(C^{*}(z))\big|^{4}\Big|\hat{I}\Big]\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[{1\over n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\big|X_{i}^{\top}C^{*}(z)X_{i}-{\rm tr}(C^{*}(z))\big|^{4}\Big|\hat{I}\Big]\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[{1\over n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big((\|X_{i}\|^{2}+m)\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\|C^{*}(z)\|_{{S}_{\infty}}\big)^{4}\Big|\hat{I}\Big]\Big]

Recalling the definition of the set
{\cal A}_{n}
in (
E.9
) it follows
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[S_{1}\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\Big[{1\over n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}=1}^{q^{\prime}}X_{ik_{1}}^{2}X_{ik_{2}}^{2}X_{ik_{4}}^{2}X_{ik_{4}}^{2}\Big]\Big|\hat{I}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq{m\over n}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}=1}^{q^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\big[X_{1k_{1}}^{2}X_{1k_{2}}^{2}X_{1k_{4}}^{2}X_{1k_{4}}^{2}\big]\lesssim{m^{5}\over n}

On the other hand, on the set
{\cal A}_{n}^{c}
,

\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\|C^{*}(z)\|\lesssim{1\over|\Im(z)|}\leq m^{1+\alpha}
and
X_{ik}^{2}\leq\delta_{n}^{2}m

while
m^{\ell}\mathbb{P}({\cal A}_{n}^{c})=o(1)
for any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
. This gives

\mathbb{E}[S_{1}\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}^{c}}]=O(m^{-\ell})

for any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
We now turn to the term
\mathbb{E}S_{2}
and introduce the notation

\displaystyle M^{*}_{n}(z):={1\over n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}^{c}}\big|X_{i}^{\top}C^{*}(z)X_{i}-{\rm tr}(C^{*}(z))\big|^{4}

It follows that

|M_{n}^{*}(z)|\lesssim\big|M_{n1}^{*}(z)|+|M_{n2}^{*}(z)|

where

\displaystyle M_{n1}^{*}(z)

\displaystyle={1\over n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}^{c}}\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{q^{\prime}}(X_{ij}^{2}-1)c_{jj}^{*}(z)\Big|^{4}

\displaystyle M_{n2}^{*}(z)

\displaystyle={1\over n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}^{c}}\Big|\sum_{j\not=j^{\prime}}^{q^{\prime}}c_{jj^{\prime}}^{*}(z)X_{ij}X_{ij^{\prime}}\Big|^{4}

We will prove that

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}|M_{n}^{*}(z)|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(r_{n}),

(E.18)

where
r_{n}=m^{3}\delta_{m}^{4}+\frac{m^{5}}{n}
,
by considering the terms
M_{n1}^{*}
and
M_{n2}^{*}
separately. Note that by Theorem
4.3
, this statement is obvious for
M_{n\ell}\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}^{c}}
. To prove (
E.18
) it is therefore sufficent to show that

(i)

For any
z\in{\cal C}_{n}
we have

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}M_{n\ell}^{*}(z)=O_{\mathbb{P}}(r_{n})~~,~~~~\ell=1,2.

(E.19)

This statement follows directly from Proposition
E.1
.

(ii)

The sequences
(r_{n}^{-1}M_{n\ell}^{*}(z)\colon z\in{\cal C}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
are stochastically equicontinuous, for which we establish

\displaystyle\sup_{n}\sup_{z_{1},z_{2}\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}{|M_{n\ell}^{*}(z_{1})-M_{n\ell}^{*}(z_{2})|^{2}\over r_{n}^{2}|z_{1}-z_{2}|^{2}}\Big]\leq K~,~~~\ell=1,2.

(E.20)

(
Billingsley,, 1968
, cf.)
. Here we assume w.l.o.g. that
z_{1}
and
z_{2}
have the real or imaginary part.

Proof of (ii) for
M_{n2}^{*}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}{|M_{n\ell}^{*}(z_{1})-M_{n\ell}^{*}(z_{2})|^{2}\over r_{n}^{2}|z_{1}-z_{2}|^{2}}\Big]={1\over r_{n}^{2}|z_{1}-z_{2}|^{2}}{1\over n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}\in\hat{I}^{c}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}\dots,j_{4}\\
j_{1}^{\prime}\dots,j_{4}^{\prime}\\
j_{\ell}\not=j_{\ell}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}^{q^{\prime}}\Big\{\big|c^{*}_{j_{1},j_{1}^{\prime}}(z_{1})\dots c^{*}_{j_{4},j_{4}^{\prime}}(z_{1})

\displaystyle~~~~~-c^{*}_{j_{1},j_{1}^{\prime}}(z_{2})\dots c^{*}_{j_{4},j_{4}^{\prime}}(z_{2})\big|^{2}X_{i_{1}j_{1}}X_{i_{1}j_{1}^{\prime}}\dots X_{i_{1}j_{4}}X_{i_{1}j_{4}^{\prime}}X_{i_{2}j_{1}}X_{i_{2}j_{1}^{\prime}}\dots X_{i_{2}j_{1}}X_{i_{2}j_{4}^{\prime}}\Big\}\Big]

We first consider the outer sum with indices
i_{1}=i_{2}\in\hat{I}^{c}
. By conditioning on
\hat{I}
and observing
\{X_{i}\colon~|i\in\hat{I}^{c}\}
and
C^{*}(z)
are stochastically independent conditionally on
\hat{I}
it follows that

\displaystyle{1\over r_{n}^{2}|z_{1}-z_{2}|^{2}}{1\over n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i_{1}\in\hat{I}^{c}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}\dots,j_{4}\\
j_{1}^{\prime}\dots,j_{4}^{\prime}\\
j_{\ell}\not=j_{\ell}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}^{q^{\prime}}\Big\{\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}\big|c^{*}_{j_{1},j_{1}^{\prime}}(z_{1})\dots c^{*}_{j_{4},j_{4}^{\prime}}(z_{1})

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~-c^{*}_{j_{1},j_{1}^{\prime}}(z_{2})\dots c^{*}_{j_{4},j_{4}^{\prime}}(z_{2})\big|^{2}X_{i_{1}j_{1}}^{2}X_{i_{1}j_{1}^{\prime}}^{2}\dots X_{i_{1}j_{4}}^{2}X_{i_{1}j_{4}^{\prime}}^{2}\Big|\hat{I}\Big]\Big\}\Big]

\displaystyle{1\over r_{n}^{2}|z_{1}-z_{2}|^{2}}{1\over n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i_{1}\in\hat{I}^{c}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}\dots,j_{4}\\
j_{1}^{\prime}\dots,j_{4}^{\prime}\\
j_{\ell}\not=j_{\ell}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}^{q^{\prime}}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}\big|c^{*}_{j_{1},j_{1}^{\prime}}(z_{1})\dots c^{*}_{j_{4},j_{4}^{\prime}}(z_{1})

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~-c^{*}_{j_{1},j_{1}^{\prime}}(z_{2})\dots c^{*}_{j_{4},j_{4}^{\prime}}(z_{2})\big|^{2}\Big|\hat{I}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[X_{i_{1}j_{1}}^{2}X_{i_{1}j_{1}^{\prime}}^{2}\dots X_{i_{1}j_{4}}^{2}X_{i_{1}j_{4}^{\prime}}^{2}\Big|\hat{I}\Big]\Big\}\Big]

A first order Taylor expansion yields

\displaystyle c^{*}_{j_{1},j_{1}^{\prime}}(z_{1})\dots

\displaystyle c^{*}_{j_{4},j_{4}^{\prime}}(z_{1})-c^{*}_{j_{1},j_{1}^{\prime}}(z_{2})\dots c^{*}_{j_{4},j_{4}^{\prime}}(z_{2})

(E.21)

\displaystyle=\Big\{{c^{*}}^{\prime}_{j_{1},j_{1}^{\prime}}(\xi_{z_{1},z_{2}}^{j_{1},j_{1}^{\prime}})c^{*}_{j_{2},j_{2}^{\prime}}(z_{2})c^{*}_{j_{3},j_{3}^{\prime}}(z_{2})c^{*}_{j_{4},j_{4}^{\prime}}(z_{2})

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ +c^{*}_{j_{1},j_{1}^{\prime}}(z_{2}){c^{*}}^{\prime}_{j_{2},j_{2}^{\prime}}(\xi_{z_{1},z_{2}}^{j_{2},j_{2}^{\prime}})c^{*}_{j_{2},j_{2}^{\prime}}(z_{2})c^{*}_{j_{4},j_{4}^{\prime}}(z_{2})

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ +c^{*}_{j_{1},j_{1}^{\prime}}(z_{2})c^{*}_{j_{2},j_{2}^{\prime}}(z_{2}){c^{*}}^{\prime}_{j_{3},j_{3}^{\prime}}(\xi_{z_{1},z_{2}}^{j_{3},j_{3}^{\prime}})c^{*}_{j_{4},j_{4}^{\prime}}(z_{2})

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ +c^{*}_{j_{1},j_{1}^{\prime}}(z_{2})c^{*}_{j_{2},j_{2}^{\prime}}(z_{2})c^{*}_{j_{3},j_{3}^{\prime}}(z_{2}){c^{*}}^{\prime}_{j_{4},j_{4}^{\prime}}(\xi_{z_{1},z_{2}}^{j_{4},j_{4}^{\prime}})\Big\}(z_{1}-z_{2})

Next, we use this expansion to derive the bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}\dots,j_{4}\\
j_{1}^{\prime}\dots,j_{4}^{\prime}\\
j_{\ell}\not=j_{\ell}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}^{q^{\prime}}\big(c^{*}_{j_{1},j_{1}^{\prime}}(z_{1})\dots c^{*}_{j_{4},j_{4}^{\prime}}(z_{1})-c^{*}_{j_{1},j_{1}^{\prime}}(z_{2})\dots c^{*}_{j_{4},j_{4}^{\prime}}(z_{2})\big)^{2}\Big|\hat{I}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[X_{i_{1}j_{1}}^{2}X_{i_{1}j_{1}^{\prime}}^{2}\dots X_{i_{1}j_{4}}^{2}X_{i_{1}j_{4}^{\prime}}^{2}\Big|\hat{I}\Big]

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~=|z_{1}-z_{2}|^{2}o(r_{n}^{2})

(E.22)

To prove this we decompose the sum in partial sums where

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[X_{i_{1}j_{1}}^{2}X_{i_{1}j_{1}^{\prime}}^{2}\dots X_{i_{1}j_{4}}^{2}X_{i_{1}j_{4}^{\prime}}^{2}\Big|\hat{I}\Big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[X_{i_{1}j_{1}}^{2}X_{i_{1}j_{1}^{\prime}}^{2}\dots X_{i_{1}j_{4}}^{2}X_{i_{1}j_{4}^{\prime}}^{2}\Big]

(E.23)

attains the same value (here the identity holds because
\hat{I}
is independent of
X_{1},\ldots X_{n}
). We first consider the case where all indices in (
E.23
) are different for which the right and side reduces to
1
. In this case we have

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}\sup_{\xi\in{\cal C}_{n}}|{c^{*}}^{\prime}_{jj^{\prime}}(\xi)|^{2}\leq\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}\sup_{\xi\in{\cal C}_{n}}\sum_{jj^{\prime}=1}^{q^{\prime}}|{c^{*}}^{\prime}_{jj^{\prime}}(\xi)|^{2}\leq m\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}\sup_{\xi\in{\cal C}_{n}}\|{C^{*}}^{\prime}(\xi)\|^{2}_{S_{\infty}}\lesssim m,

where the last inequality follows from the fact that
for the matrices under consideration the spectral norm of the derivative of the matrix
C^{*}

is uniformly bounded on
{\cal C}_{n}
on the set
{\cal A}_{n}
(see Lemma
E.10
and (
E.13
)). Therefore, we obtain for the corresponding partial sum in (
E.22
) the bound (up to a constant)

\displaystyle m\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}j_{2},j_{3}\\
j_{1}^{\prime}j_{2}^{\prime},j_{3}^{\prime}\\
j_{\ell}\not=j_{\ell}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}^{q^{\prime}}|c_{j_{1}j_{1}^{\prime}}(z)|^{2}|c_{j_{2}j_{2}^{\prime}}(z)|^{2}|c_{j_{3}j_{3}^{\prime}}(z)|^{2}\leq m\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\|C^{*}(z)\|_{S_{2}}^{6}\lesssim m^{4}

(E.24)

The remaining partial sums can be treated in the same way using the bound
\mathbb{E}[X_{ij}^{2k}]\leq E[X_{ij}^{4}](\delta_{n}\sqrt{m})^{2k-4}
for
k\geq 2
and
\sum_{ij=1}^{q^{\prime}}|c^{*}_{ij}(z)|^{2k}\leq\|C^{*}(z)\|_{{\cal S}_{2}}^{2k}\lesssim m^{k}
on
{\cal A}_{n}
, while respecting the reduced number of summands.

Proof of (ii) for
M_{n1}^{*}
:
Follows by similar but even simpler arguments.

∎

E.2
Bootstrap version of the Martingale-CLT

Theorem E.4
(Bootstrap Martingale-CLT).

Let
(X_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
be an iid-sequence,
(\Pi_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be some further sequence of random variables independent of
(X_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
, and
m\leq n
with
m=m(n)\rightarrow\infty
. Conditional on
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
, let

X_{1}^{*},\dots,X_{m}^{*}\overset{iid}{\sim}\hat{\mathbb{P}}_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\delta_{X_{i}}

denote the’
m
out of
n
’ bootstrap sample. Suppose that conditional on
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
and
\Pi_{n}
,

Y_{n,1}^{*},\dots,Y_{n,m}^{*}

is a real square integrable martingale difference sequence with respect to the bootstrap canonical filtration
(\mathcal{F}_{n,k}^{*})_{k=1}^{m}
with
\mathcal{F}_{n,k}^{*}=\sigma(X_{1}^{*},\dots,X_{k}^{*}\arrowvert X_{1},\dots,X_{n},\Pi_{n})
.
Assume that, as
n\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big(\arrowvert Y_{n,k}^{*}\arrowvert^{2}\big\arrowvert\mathcal{F}_{n,k-1}^{*}\big)\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}\sigma^{2}

(E.25)

for some constant
\sigma^{2}>0
and

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big[\arrowvert Y_{n,k}^{*}\arrowvert^{2}1\hskip-3.87495pt1_{\{\arrowvert Y_{n,k}^{*}\arrowvert\geq\varepsilon\}}\Big]\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}0

(E.26)

for each
\varepsilon>0
. Then, as
n\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{m}Y_{n,k}^{*}\bigg\arrowvert X_{1},\dots,X_{n},\Pi_{n}\bigg)\Longrightarrow\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})\ \ \text{in probability}.

(E.27)

Preliminary, we assume that there exists some constant
c>0
with

\displaystyle\sup_{n}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big(Y_{n,k}^{*2}\big\arrowvert Y_{1}^{*},\dots,Y_{k-1}^{*}\big)\leq c.

(E.28)

Claim I.
With

Z_{n}^{*}=\sum_{k=1}^{m}Y_{n,k}^{*},

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in K}\Big\arrowvert\mathbb{E}^{*}\exp(itZ_{n}^{*})-\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}t^{2}\sigma^{2}\Big)\Big\arrowvert\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}0

(E.29)

for any compact subset
K\subset\mathbb{R}
.

Proof of Claim I.

The proof follows the lines in the proof of the classical martingale CLT, replacing all expectations by conditional expectations
\mathbb{E}^{*}
and the canonical filtration correspondingly. However, we state here locally uniform stochastic convergence rather than pointwise stochastic convergence of the characteristic functions, which requires some extra care with the transfer of arguments.

Write

\displaystyle\sigma_{n,l}^{*2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{*}(Y_{n,l}^{*2}\arrowvert Y_{1}^{*},\dots,Y_{l-1}^{*})\ \ \text{for }2\leq l\leq m,\ \ \sigma_{n,1}^{*2}=\mathbb{E}^{*}Y_{n,1}^{*2},

\displaystyle\Sigma_{n,l}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{l}\sigma_{n,k}^{*2}\ \ \text{ for }0\leq l\leq m,\ \text{and}

\displaystyle Z_{n,l}^{*}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{l}Y_{n,k}^{*}\ \ \text{for }0\leq l\leq m.

Let
K\subset\mathbb{R}
be compact. Then

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in K}\bigg\arrowvert\mathbb{E}^{*}

\displaystyle\bigg[\exp\Big(itZ_{n}^{*}\Big)-\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}t^{2}\sigma^{2}\Big)\bigg]\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in K}\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\bigg\arrowvert 1-\exp\Big(\frac{1}{2}t^{2}\Sigma_{n,m}\Big)\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}t^{2}\sigma^{2}\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sup_{t\in K}\bigg\arrowvert\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\exp\Big(\frac{1}{2}t^{2}\Sigma_{n,m}\Big)\exp\Big(itZ_{n}^{*}\Big)-1\bigg]\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle=:A_{K}+B_{K}.

Choose some
0\leq t_{K}\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying
-t_{K}\leq x\leq t_{K}
for all
x\in K
.
As
\Sigma_{n,m}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}\sigma^{2}
by assumption and (
E.28
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}A_{K}\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg(\exp\Big(\frac{1}{2}t_{K}^{2}\arrowvert\Sigma_{n,m}-\sigma^{2}\arrowvert\Big)-1\bigg)\rightarrow 0.

As concerns
B_{K}
,

\displaystyle B_{K}

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\in K}\bigg\arrowvert\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\exp\Big(itZ_{n,{k-1}}^{*}\Big)\exp\Big(\frac{1}{2}t^{2}\Sigma_{n,k}\Big)\bigg(\exp(itY_{n,k}^{*})-\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}t^{2}\sigma_{n,k}^{*2}\Big)\bigg)\bigg]\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big(\frac{1}{2}t_{K}^{2}c\Big)\sup_{t\in K}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\bigg\arrowvert\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg(\exp\big(itY_{n,k}^{*}\big)-\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}t^{2}\sigma_{n,k}^{*2}\Big)\bigg\arrowvert\mathcal{F}_{n,k-1}^{*}\bigg)\bigg\arrowvert\bigg].

Still assuming the temporary condition (
E.29
), it remains to prove that the latter expression converges to
0
in probability. Taylor’s approximation reveals

\displaystyle\exp\Big(itY_{n,k}^{*}\Big)=1+itY_{n,k}^{*}-\frac{1}{2}t^{2}Y_{n,k}^{*2}+\theta_{n,k}^{*}(t)

(E.30)

with

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in K}\arrowvert\theta_{n,k}^{*}(t)\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in K}\min\Big\{\arrowvert tY_{n,k}^{*}\arrowvert^{3},\arrowvert tY_{n,k}^{*}\arrowvert^{2}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq(t_{K}^{2}+t_{K}^{3})\Big(Y_{n,k}^{*2}1\hskip-3.87495pt1_{\{\arrowvert Y_{n,k}^{*}\arrowvert\geq\varepsilon\}}+\varepsilon Y_{n,k}^{*2}\Big)

for any
\varepsilon>0
as well as

\displaystyle\exp\Big(\frac{1}{2}t^{2}\sigma_{n,k}^{*2}\Big)=1-\frac{1}{2}t^{2}\sigma_{n,k}^{*2}+\theta_{n,k}^{\prime}(t)

(E.31)

with

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in K}\arrowvert\theta_{n,k}^{\prime}(t)\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in K}\Big(\frac{1}{2}t^{2}\sigma_{n,k}^{*2}\Big)^{2}\exp\Big(\frac{1}{2}t^{2}\sigma_{n,k}^{*2}\Big)\leq t_{K}^{4}\sigma_{n,k}^{*4}\exp\Big(\frac{1}{2}t_{K}^{2}c\Big).

Therefore, with

c_{K}=t_{K}^{2}+t_{K}^{3}+t_{K}^{4}\exp\Big(\frac{1}{2}t_{K}^{2}c\Big),

we arrive at

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in K}\sum_{k=1}^{m}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\bigg\arrowvert\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg(\exp\big(itY_{n,k}^{*}\big)-\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}t^{2}\sigma_{k,n}^{*2}\Big)\bigg\arrowvert\mathcal{F}_{n,k-1}^{*}\bigg)\bigg\arrowvert\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq c_{K}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\bigg[\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big(Y_{n,k}^{*2}1\hskip-3.87495pt1_{\{\arrowvert Y_{n,k}^{*}\arrowvert\geq\varepsilon\}}\Big)+\varepsilon\mathbb{E}^{*}(\sigma_{n,k}^{*2})+\mathbb{E}^{*}(\sigma_{n,k}^{*4})\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq c_{K}\bigg[\varepsilon c+c\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big(\max_{k=1,\dots,n}\sigma_{n,k}^{*2}\Big)+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big(Y_{n,k}^{*2}1\hskip-3.87495pt1_{\{\arrowvert Y_{n,j}^{*}\arrowvert\geq\varepsilon\}}\Big)\bigg].

(E.32)

Because of
\sigma_{n,l}^{*2}\leq\varepsilon^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}^{*}(Y_{n,k}^{*2}1\hskip-3.87495pt1_{\{\arrowvert Y_{n,l}^{*}\arrowvert\geq\varepsilon\}})
for any
1\leq l\leq m
, (
E.26
) reveals that (
E.32
) is upper bounded by

\displaystyle c_{K}(\varepsilon c+c\varepsilon^{2})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Since
\varepsilon>0
was chosen arbitrarily, this proves (
E.29
).

Claim II.
The sequence
(Z_{n}^{*})
is tight in probability, i.e. for any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists some compact subset
K_{\varepsilon}\subset\mathbb{R}
, such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathbb{P}\big(Z_{n}^{*}\not\in K_{\varepsilon}\big\arrowvert X_{1},\dots,X_{n},\Pi_{n}\big)>\varepsilon\Big)=0.

(E.33)

Proof of Claim II.
Using the identity

\displaystyle\int_{-u}^{u}\big(1-\exp(itx)\big)\dd t=2u-\frac{\exp(iux)-\exp(-iux)}{ix}=2u-\frac{2\sin(ux)}{x},

we obtain by the Theorem of Fubini

\displaystyle\frac{1}{u}

\displaystyle\int_{-u}^{u}\big(1-\mathbb{E}^{*}\exp(itZ_{n}^{*})\big)\dd t

\displaystyle=\int\Big(2-\frac{2\sin(ux)}{ux}\Big)\dd\mathbb{P}^{Z_{n}^{*}\arrowvert X_{1},\dots,X_{n},\Pi_{n}}(x)

\displaystyle\geq 2\int_{\{\arrowvert x\arrowvert\geq 2/u\}}\Big(1-\Big\arrowvert\frac{\sin(ux)}{ux}\Big\arrowvert\Big)\dd\mathbb{P}^{Z_{n}^{*}\arrowvert X_{1},\dots,X_{n},\Pi_{n}}(x)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\Big(\arrowvert Z_{n}^{*}\arrowvert\geq\frac{2}{u}\Big\arrowvert X_{1},\dots,X_{n},\Pi_{n}\Big),

where we used
\arrowvert\sin(v)/v\arrowvert\leq 1
in the first inequality and
\arrowvert\sin(ux)\arrowvert\leq 1
in the last line. Fix now
\varepsilon>0
and choose
u>0
such that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{u}\int_{-u}^{u}\bigg(1-\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}t^{2}\sigma^{2}\Big)\bigg)\dd t\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}.

With
K_{\varepsilon}=[-2/u,2/u]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathbb{P}\big(Z_{n}^{*}\not\in K_{\varepsilon}\big\arrowvert X_{1},\dots,X_{n},\Pi_{n}\big)>\varepsilon\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\bigg(\frac{1}{u}\int_{-u}^{u}\Big(1-\mathbb{E}^{*}\exp(itZ_{n}^{*})\Big)\dd t>\varepsilon\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\bigg(\frac{1}{u}\int_{-u}^{u}\Big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\exp(itZ_{n}^{*})-\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}t^{2}\sigma^{2}\Big)\Big)\dd t>\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\bigg).

The last expression is equal to zero by (
E.29
), which proves (
E.33
).

Claim III.
For any bounded Lipschitz function
f
,

\displaystyle\int f(Z_{n}^{*})\dd\hat{\mathbb{Q}}_{n}\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}\int f\dd\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})\ \ \ (n\rightarrow\infty),

(E.34)

where
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}_{n}
denotes the random distribution
\mathbb{P}(~\cdot~\arrowvert X_{1},\dots,X_{n},\Pi_{n})
.

Proof of Claim III.

For any
\varepsilon>0
, fix
K_{\varepsilon}=[-c_{\varepsilon},c_{\varepsilon}]
which satisfies
\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})(K_{\varepsilon})\geq 1-\varepsilon
and (
E.33
). Next, for any bounded Lipschitz function
f
and any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists some bounded Lipschitz function
\tilde{f}_{\varepsilon}
with

f_{\arrowvert K_{\varepsilon}}=\tilde{f}_{\varepsilon\arrowvert K_{\varepsilon}}\ \ \ \text{and}\ \ \ \tilde{f}_{\varepsilon}(x)=0\ \text{for all}\ x\in[-c_{\varepsilon}-\Arrowvert f\Arrowvert_{\sup},c_{\varepsilon}+\Arrowvert f\Arrowvert_{\sup}]^{c},

and

\displaystyle\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\int f(Z_{n}^{*})\dd\hat{\mathbb{Q}}_{n}-\int f\dd\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq 2\Arrowvert f\Arrowvert_{\sup}\hat{\mathbb{Q}}_{n}\big(Z_{n}^{*}\not\in K_{\varepsilon}\big)+2\Arrowvert f\Arrowvert_{\sup}\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})\big(K_{\varepsilon}^{c}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\bigg\arrowvert\int\tilde{f}_{\varepsilon}(Z_{n}^{*})\dd\hat{\mathbb{Q}}_{n}-\int\tilde{f}_{\varepsilon}\dd\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\arrowvert\int\tilde{f}_{\varepsilon}(Z_{n}^{*})\dd\hat{\mathbb{Q}}_{n}-\int\tilde{f}_{\varepsilon}\dd\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})\bigg\arrowvert+4\Arrowvert f\Arrowvert_{\sup}\varepsilon+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Identifying the endpoint
-c_{\varepsilon}-\Arrowvert f\Arrowvert_{\sup}
with
c_{\varepsilon}+\Arrowvert f\Arrowvert_{\sup}
yields the torus
T_{\varepsilon}
on which
f_{\varepsilon}
is continuous. As the complex linear combinations of the monomials

m_{j}:x\mapsto\exp\left(i\frac{2\pi}{2c_{\varepsilon}+2}jx\right),\ j\in\mathbb{Z},

restricted to
T_{\varepsilon}
form a point-separating self-adjoint
\mathbb{C}
-algebra of functions on
T_{\varepsilon}
which includes constants, the Stone-Weierstraß theorem reveals that they are dense in the space of continuous complex functions on
T_{\varepsilon}
with respect to the topology of uniform convergence. Hence, there exists some linear combination
P_{f,\varepsilon}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\alpha_{j}m_{j}
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{-c_{\varepsilon}-\Arrowvert f\Arrowvert_{\sup}\leq x\leq c_{\varepsilon}+\Arrowvert f\Arrowvert_{\sup}}\Big\arrowvert\tilde{f}_{\varepsilon}(x)-P_{f,\varepsilon}(x)\Big\arrowvert<\varepsilon.

(E.35)

Moreover, since
\tilde{f}_{\varepsilon}
is bounded in absolute value by
1
and
P_{f,\varepsilon}
is
(2c_{\varepsilon}+2)
-periodic, (
E.35
) reveals
\Arrowvert P_{f,\varepsilon}\Arrowvert_{\sup}\leq\Arrowvert f\Arrowvert_{\sup}+\varepsilon
.
Claim I and Claim II then imply

\displaystyle\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\int\tilde{f}_{\varepsilon}(Z_{n}^{*})\dd\hat{\mathbb{Q}}_{n}-\int\tilde{f}_{\varepsilon}\dd\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\int_{-c_{\varepsilon}-\Arrowvert f\Arrowvert_{\sup}}^{c_{\varepsilon}+\Arrowvert f\Arrowvert_{\sup}}\Big\arrowvert\tilde{f}_{\varepsilon}(x)-P_{f,\varepsilon}(x)\Big\arrowvert\dd\Big(\hat{\mathbb{Q}}_{n}^{Z_{n}^{*}}+\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+(\Arrowvert f\Arrowvert_{\sup}+\varepsilon)\hat{\mathbb{Q}}_{n}\big(Z_{n}^{*}\not\in K_{\varepsilon}\big)+(\Arrowvert f\Arrowvert_{\sup}+\varepsilon)\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})\big(K_{\varepsilon}^{c}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\bigg\arrowvert\int P_{f,\varepsilon}(Z_{n}^{*})\dd\hat{\mathbb{Q}}_{n}-\int P_{f,\varepsilon}\dd\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon+2(\Arrowvert f\Arrowvert_{\sup}+\varepsilon)\varepsilon+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Summarizing,

\displaystyle\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\int f(Z_{n}^{*})\dd\hat{\mathbb{Q}}_{n}-\int f\dd\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})\bigg\arrowvert\leq 6\Arrowvert f\Arrowvert_{\sup}\varepsilon+2\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Since
\varepsilon>0
is arbitrary, this proves (
E.34
).

Claim IV.
As
n\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle d_{BL}\Big(\mathcal{L}\big(Z_{n}^{*}\big\arrowvert X_{1},\dots,X_{n},\Pi_{n}\big),\,\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})\Big)\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}0.

(E.36)

Proof of Claim IV.
As in the proof of claim III, for any
\varepsilon>0
, fix
K_{\varepsilon}=[-c_{\varepsilon},c_{\varepsilon}]
which satisfies
\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})(K_{\varepsilon})\geq 1-\varepsilon
and (
E.33
).
Denote the closed unit ball of bounded Lipschitz functions as introduced in Subsection
A.1
by

B=\big\{f\in BL:\Arrowvert f\Arrowvert_{BL}\leq 1\big\}

and define

B_{K_{\varepsilon}}=\big\{g:K_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:g=f_{\arrowvert K_{\varepsilon}}\ \text{for some}\ f\in B\big\}.

Then the set
B_{K_{\varepsilon}}
is closed with respect to the topology of uniform convergence and therefore compact by the Arzelà-Ascoli theorem. Hence, for any
\varepsilon>0
, there exist
N\in N
and
f_{1},\dots,f_{N}\in B
, such that for any
f\in B
, there exists some
g_{f}\in\{f_{1},\dots,f_{N}\}
with

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in K_{\varepsilon}}\arrowvert f(x)-g_{f}(x)\arrowvert<\varepsilon.

As a consequence,

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in B}\bigg\arrowvert\int

\displaystyle(f-g_{f})(\dd\hat{\mathbb{Q}}_{n}^{Z_{n}^{*}}-\dd\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}))\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{f\in B}\int_{K_{\varepsilon}}\arrowvert f-g_{f}\arrowvert\dd(\hat{\mathbb{Q}}_{n}^{Z_{n}^{*}}+\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}))+2\hat{\mathbb{Q}}_{n}^{Z_{n}^{*}}(K_{\varepsilon}^{c})+2\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})(K_{\varepsilon}^{c})

\displaystyle\leq 6\varepsilon+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

and therefore,

\displaystyle d_{BL}

\displaystyle\Big(\mathcal{L}\big(Z_{n}^{*}\big\arrowvert X_{1},\dots,X_{n},\Pi_{n}\big),\,\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j=1,\dots,N}\bigg\arrowvert\int f_{j}(\dd\hat{\mathbb{Q}}_{n}^{Z_{n}^{*}}-\dd\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}))\bigg\arrowvert+6\varepsilon+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

\displaystyle=6\varepsilon+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

by (
E.34
). As
\varepsilon>0
was arbitrary, this proves (
E.36
).

To remove assumption (
E.28
), the same argument as in the proof of the classical martingale-CLT can be applied.
∎

E.3
Proof of Proposition
D.3

E.3.1
Weak convergence of finite dimensional distributions in probability

Recall that
\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}
denotes
the conditional expectation operator corresponding to
\hat{\mathbb{P}}_{n}
with respect to the
\sigma
-field generated by
X_{1}^{*},\dots,X_{j}^{*}
(conditional on
\Pi_{n}
)
Similar calculations as in Section 2 (p. 569-570) of
Bai and Silverstein, (2004)

and an application of Proposition
E.1
for the matrices
L_{n}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}L_{n}
and
L_{n}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-2}L_{n}

yield for
z\in\mathcal{C}_{n}

\displaystyle\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z)-\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z)\big]

\displaystyle:=q\big(m_{\mu^{\widehat{\Sigma}_{n}^{*}}}(z)-\mathbb{E}^{*}m_{\mu^{\widehat{\Sigma}_{n}^{*}}}(z)\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}Y_{j}^{*}+O_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\delta_{m}^{2}+\sqrt{m^{2}\over n}\Big)=\sum_{j=1}^{m}Y_{j}^{*}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1))~,

where

\displaystyle Y_{j}^{*}

\displaystyle=-(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}-\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*})\Big(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z)\delta_{j}^{*}(z)-\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z)^{2}\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z)\frac{1}{m}\tr\big(L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{j}^{*}(z)^{-2}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=-(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}-\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*})\Big(\frac{\dd}{\dd z}\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z)\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z)\Big).

For example, by the algebraic manipulations on page 569 in this reference we obtain

\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z)=\sum_{i=1}^{m}Y_{j}^{*}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}Z_{j}^{*}

where

Z_{j}^{*}=(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}-\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*})\big[\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z)(\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z)\delta_{j}^{*}(z)-{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z)r_{j}^{*\top}D_{j}^{-2}(z)r_{j}^{*}\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z)^{2})\big].

The
L^{2}
-norm of the first term is now estimated as follows

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{m}(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}-\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*})(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z)\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z)\delta_{j}^{*}(z))\Big|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\big|(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}-\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*})(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z)\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z)\delta_{j}^{*}(z))\big|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 4\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\big|\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z)\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z)\delta_{j}^{*}(z)\big|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\delta_{m}^{2}+\sqrt{m^{2}\over n}\Big)~,

(E.37)

where the last estimate follows from Proposition
E.1

and the bounds
\arrowvert\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z)\arrowvert\leq\arrowvert z\arrowvert/\Im(z)
.
The second term can be estimated by similar arguments and is of order

O_{\mathbb{P}}(\delta_{m}^{4}+m^{2}/n)
.
Note that one crucial difference to the analysis of
Bai and Silverstein, (2004)
is now caused by the fact that the random variables
\varepsilon_{j}^{*}
and
\delta_{j}^{*}
are not centered anymore with respect to
\mathbb{E}_{X_{j}^{*}}^{*}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{j}^{*}}^{*}\big(r_{j}^{*\top}D_{j}^{*}(z)^{-k}r_{j}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\tr\Big(L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{j}^{*}(z)^{-k}\Big)\not=\frac{1}{m}\tr\Big(L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{j}^{*}(z)^{-k}\Big),\ \ k=1,2.

In view of the limiting distribution result, it is therefore sufficient to study linear combinations

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{r}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\alpha_{i}Y_{j}^{*}(z_{i})\ \ \text{with }\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{r}\in\mathbb{C},\ r\in\mathbb{N},

due to the Cramér-Wold device (since the real parts of these linear combinations are running over all real linear combinations of
\Re(\sum_{j=1}^{m}Y_{j}^{*}(z_{i})),\Im(\sum_{j=1}^{m}Y_{j}(z_{i}))
,
i=1,\dots,r
, as
\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{r}
varies over
\mathbb{C}
).
Note furthermore that it is sufficient to consider the case
\Im(z_{i})>0
(
i=1,\ldots,r
), because the distribution of any
C(\mathcal{C},\mathbb{R}^{2})
-valued random variable
Z
is uniquely determined by its finite dimensional distributions
\mathcal{L}(Z(z_{1}),\dots,Z(z_{k}))
with
z_{1},\dots,z_{k}
belonging to a dense subset of
\mathcal{C}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
For this purpose, we shall prove that the conditions of
Theorem
E.4
in the online supplement
are
satisfied for

\Re\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{r}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\alpha_{i}Y_{j}^{*}(z_{i})\Big).

By
the bounds
\arrowvert\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z)\arrowvert\leq\arrowvert z\arrowvert/\Im(z)
and
the estimate

\Big\arrowvert\frac{1}{m}\tr\big(L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{j}^{*}(z)^{-2}\big)\Big\arrowvert\leq\frac{q}{m}\Arrowvert L_{n}\Arrowvert^{2}_{S_{\infty}}\cdot\Arrowvert D_{j}^{*}(z)^{-2}\Arrowvert_{S_{\infty}}\leq c(1+o(1))\frac{\Arrowvert L_{n}\Arrowvert^{2}_{S_{\infty}}}{\Im(z)^{2}},

we find by Proposition
E.1

(with
p=2
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\arrowvert Y_{j}^{*}(z)\arrowvert^{4}

\displaystyle\leq K\Big(\frac{\arrowvert z\arrowvert^{4}}{\Im(z)^{4}}\mathbb{E}\arrowvert\delta_{j}^{*}(z)\arrowvert^{4}+\frac{\arrowvert z\arrowvert^{8}}{\Im(z)^{16}}c^{4}(1+o(1))\mathbb{E}\arrowvert\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z)\arrowvert^{4}\Big)=\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{\delta_{m}^{4}}{m}+\frac{m}{n}\Big)

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg(\bigg\arrowvert\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}Y_{j}^{*}(z_{i})\bigg\arrowvert^{2}I\bigg\{\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}Y_{j}^{*}(z_{i})\arrowvert\geq\varepsilon\bigg\}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert\sum_{j=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}Y_{j}^{*}(z_{i})\bigg\arrowvert^{4}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Big(\delta_{m}^{4}+\frac{m^{2}}{n}\Big)=o(1)

(E.38)

as
n\rightarrow\infty
, and condition (
E.26
) of Theorem
E.4
is fulfilled.

In order to verify condition (
E.25
), it is sufficient to show that for
z_{1},z_{2}
with
\Im(z_{1})\not=0
,
\Im(z_{2})\not=0
,

\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}^{*}_{j-1}Y_{j}^{*}(z_{1})Y_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}\begin{cases}\frac{1}{3}\frac{(\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0})^{\prime\prime\prime}(z_{1})}{(\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0})^{\prime}(z_{1})}-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{(\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0})^{\prime\prime}(z_{1})}{(\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0})^{\prime}(z_{1})}\Big)^{2}&\text{ if }z_{1}=z_{2}\\
~&\\
2\frac{(\underline{m}_{{c,H}}^{0})^{\prime}(z_{1})(\underline{m}_{{c,H}}^{0})^{\prime}(z_{2})}{\big(\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0}(z_{1})-\underline{m}_{{c,H}}^{0}(z_{2})\big)^{2}}-\frac{2}{(z_{1}-z_{2})^{2}}&\text{ if }z_{1}\not=z_{2}\end{cases}

(E.39)

(note that
\overline{Y_{j}^{*}(z)}=Y_{j}^{*}(\bar{z})
).
By the theorem of dominated convergence,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial z_{2}\partial z_{1}}\eqref{eq: 3.10}=\eqref{eq: 3.11}

with

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\Big[(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}-\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*})\big(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\big)(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}-\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*})\big(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\big)\Big].

(E.40)

As for the classical CLT of linear spectral statistics, it follows from Vitali’s convergence theorem that the convergence of (
E.39
) in probability follows from the corresponding stochastic convergence of (
E.40
).
For analyzing (
E.40
) we shall prove the following claims.

Claim I.

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\Big[(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}-\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*})\big(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\big)(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}-\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*})\big(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\big)\Big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\Big[\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\big)\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\big)\Big]+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(E.41)

Claim II.

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\Big[\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\big)\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\big)\Big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}b_{n}^{*}(z_{1})b_{n}^{*}(z_{2})\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\Big[\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\big)\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\big)\Big]+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(E.42)

Claim III.

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}

\displaystyle\,b_{n}^{*}(z_{1})b_{n}^{*}(z_{2})\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\big[\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\big)\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\big)\big]

\displaystyle=2b_{n}^{*}(z_{1})b_{n}^{*}(z_{2})\frac{1}{m^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\tr\Big(L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}\big)L_{n}\Big)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

In order to prove stochastic convergence and to determine the limit in probability of the right-hand side in Claim III, we shall prove the representation

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m^{2}}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}\tr\Big(L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}\big)L_{n}\Big)

\displaystyle=a_{n}(z_{1},z_{2})\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Big(1-\frac{j-1}{m}a_{n}(z_{1},z_{2})\Big)^{-1}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

for some function
a_{n}(z_{1},z_{2})
that will be specified in Claim VI. This is the most involved part of the proof. Claims IV and V are intermediate steps on this way. For this purpose, we
recall the notation
of
D_{ij}^{*}(z)
,
\beta_{ij}^{*}(z)
and
b_{1}^{*}(z)
in (
A.10
),
(
A.11
), and (
A.12
), respectively, which will be used intensively in the following discussion.

Claim IV.
There exists some constant
K>0
, such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and any
j\leq m

\displaystyle\tr

\displaystyle\Big(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \times\bigg[1{-}\frac{j-1}{m^{2}}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})b_{1}^{*}(z_{2})\tr\bigg(\Big(z_{2}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{2})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}

\displaystyle\hskip 128.0374pt\times\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\tr\bigg[\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big(z_{2}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{2})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\bigg]

(E.43)

\displaystyle\hskip 341.43306pt~+R(z_{1},z_{2})

with
\mathbb{E}\arrowvert R(z_{1},z_{2})\arrowvert\leq K\sqrt{m}
.

Claim V.
Recall the notation
of
\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}

in (
B.27
). For any
j\leq m
,

\displaystyle\tr\Big(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\bigg[1-\frac{j-1}{m^{2}}\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z_{1})\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z_{2})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\cdot\tr\Big((I+\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z_{2})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}(I+\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{z_{1}z_{2}}\tr\Big((I+\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z_{2})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}(I+\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)+R^{\prime}(z_{1},z_{2})

(E.44)

with
\arrowvert R^{\prime}(z_{1},z_{2})\arrowvert=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{m})
.

Claim VI:
We shall conclude the stochastic convergence in (
E.39
):

\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}^{*}_{j-1}Y_{j}^{*}(z_{1})Y_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}\begin{cases}\frac{1}{3}\frac{(\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0})^{\prime\prime\prime}(z_{1})}{(\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0})^{\prime}(z_{1})}-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{(\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0})^{\prime\prime}(z_{1})}{(\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0})^{\prime}(z_{1})}\Big)^{2}&\text{ if }z_{1}=z_{2}\\
~&\\
2\frac{(\underline{m}_{{c,H}}^{0})^{\prime}(z_{1})(\underline{m}_{{c,H}}^{0})^{\prime}(z_{2})}{\big(\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0}(z_{1})-\underline{m}_{{c,H}}^{0}(z_{2})\big)^{2}}-\frac{2}{(z_{1}-z_{2})^{2}}&\text{ if }z_{1}\not=z_{2}.\end{cases}

Note that the expression for
z_{2}=z_{2}
is the continuous extrapolation of the one for
z_{1}\not=z_{2}
for the removable singularities at
z_{1}=z_{2}
.

Proofs of Claim I – Claim VI

Proof of Claim I.

Due to the identity

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}

\displaystyle\,\Big[(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}-\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*})\big(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\big)(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}-\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*})\big(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\big)\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\Big[\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\big)\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad-\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\big(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\big)\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\big(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\big),

the claim follows if

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\big(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\big)\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\big(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\big)\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}0.

Employing the operator identity
\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}=\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\mathbb{E}_{X_{j}^{*}}^{*}
, the independence of
\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z)
and
D_{j}^{*}(z)
from
X_{j}^{*}
and the bound

\arrowvert\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z)\arrowvert\leq\frac{\arrowvert z\arrowvert}{\Im(z)},

we deduce
with
A_{j}(z)=\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\big(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z)D_{j}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big)
and
Lemma
E.6
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\big(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\big)\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\big(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\big)\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\big(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\mathbb{E}_{X_{j}^{*}}^{*}\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\big)\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\big(\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\mathbb{E}_{X_{j}^{*}}^{*}\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\big)\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\Big\arrowvert\mathbb{E}_{X_{j}^{*}}^{*}\big(r_{j}^{*\top}A_{j}(z_{1})r_{j}^{*}-{1\over m}\tr A_{j}(z_{1})\big)\mathbb{E}_{X_{j}^{*}}^{*}\big(r_{j}^{*\top}A_{j}(z_{2})r_{j}^{*}-{1\over m}\tr A_{j}(z_{2})\big)\Big\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq K(z_{1},z_{2})\Big({\delta_{m}^{2}m\over\sqrt{n}}+\frac{m^{2}}{n}\Big)

(E.45)

Proof of Claim II.

Inserting the conditional expectation operator
\mathbb{E}^{*}
, the proof follows by representing the difference as a martingale difference sum and Burkholder’s inequality with the exponent
2
and Lemma
E.8
.

Proof of Claim III.

Since
\arrowvert b_{n}^{*}(z)\arrowvert\leq\arrowvert z\arrowvert/\Im(z)
,
\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}D_{j}^{*}(z)^{-1}=\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}D_{j}^{*}(z)^{-1}
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert\sum_{j=1}^{m}

\displaystyle\bigg\{\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\Big[\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\big)\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad-\frac{2}{m^{2}}\tr\Big(L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}\big)L_{n}^{\top}\Big)\bigg\}\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\Big[\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\big)\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(\varepsilon_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad-\frac{2}{m^{2}}\tr\Big(L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}\big)L_{n}^{\top}\Big)\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert\mathbb{E}_{X_{j}^{*}}^{*}\bigg[\big(X_{j}^{*\top}A_{j}(z_{1})X_{j}^{*}-\tr A_{j}(z_{1}))\big(X_{j}^{*\top}A_{j}(z_{2})X_{j}^{*}-\tr A_{j}(z_{2}))

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~-2\tr(A_{j}(z_{1})A_{j}(z_{2}))\bigg]\bigg\arrowvert

with

A_{j}(z_{k})=\frac{1}{m}L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{k})^{-1}\big)L_{n},\ \ k=1,2,

the proof is an immediate consequence of Lemma
E.7
.

Proof of Claim IV.

The algebraic manipulations in
Bai and Silverstein, (2004)
on page 572 provide the representation

\displaystyle D_{j}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}

\displaystyle=-\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}+b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})A^{*}(z_{1})+B^{*}(z_{1})+C^{*}(z_{1})

(E.46)

with

\displaystyle A^{*}(z_{1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq m\\
i\not=j\end{subarray}}\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}\Big(r_{i}^{*}{r_{i}^{*}}^{\top}-\frac{1}{m}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1},

\displaystyle B^{*}(z_{1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq m\\
i\not=j\end{subarray}}\big(\beta_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})-b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})\big)\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}r_{i}^{*}{r_{i}^{*}}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}

and

\displaystyle C^{*}(z_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq m\\
i\not=j\end{subarray}}\Big(D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}-D_{j}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\Big).

In order to prove Claim IV, we establish the following steps.

(i)

If a possibly random
q\times q
-matrix
M
satisfies
\Arrowvert M\Arrowvert_{S_{\infty}}\leq c
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big\arrowvert\tr\big(B^{*}(z_{1})M\big)\big\arrowvert\leq Kc\frac{\arrowvert z_{1}\arrowvert^{2}(1+m/(q\Im(z_{1})))}{\Im(z_{1})^{5}}\sqrt{m}.

(ii)

If a possibly random
q\times q
-matrix
M
satisfies
\Arrowvert M\Arrowvert_{S_{\infty}}\leq c
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big\arrowvert\tr\big(C^{*}(z_{1})M\big)\big\arrowvert\leq Kc\frac{\arrowvert z_{1}\arrowvert(1+m/(q\Im(z_{1})))}{\Im(z_{1})^{3}}.

(iii)

We have

\displaystyle\tr\Big(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}

\displaystyle\big(A^{*}(z_{1})\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)

\displaystyle=\tr\Big(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(\check{A}^{*}(z_{1})\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)+\check{R}(z_{1},z_{2}),

with

\displaystyle\check{A}^{*}(z_{1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq i<j}\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}\Big(r_{i}^{*}{r_{i}^{*}}^{\top}-\frac{1}{m}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}

and
\mathbb{E}\arrowvert\check{R}(z_{1},z_{2})\arrowvert\leq K(z_{1},z_{2})\sqrt{m}
.

(iv)

Moreover,

\displaystyle\tr\Big(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(\check{A}^{*}(z_{1})\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)=A_{1}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})+R^{\prime\prime}(z_{1},z_{2}),

where
\mathbb{E}\arrowvert R^{\prime\prime}(z_{1},z_{2})\arrowvert\leq K\sqrt{m}
and

\displaystyle A_{1}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=-\sum_{1\leq i<j}\beta_{ij}^{*}(z_{2}){r_{i}^{*}}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\Big(D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\Big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}r_{i}^{*}{r_{i}^{*}}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\times L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}r_{i}^{*}.

(v)

Each summand of
A_{1}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})
satisfies the approximation

\displaystyle\beta_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})

\displaystyle{r_{i}^{*}}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\Big(D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\Big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}r_{i}^{*}{r_{i}^{*}}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\times L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}r_{i}^{*}

\displaystyle=b_{1}^{*}(z_{2})\frac{1}{m^{2}}\tr\Big[\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\Big(D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\Big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\times\tr\bigg[D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+R^{\prime\prime\prime}(z_{1},z_{2})

with
\mathbb{E}\arrowvert R^{\prime\prime\prime}(z_{1},z_{2})\arrowvert\leq Km^{-1/2}
.

Having established these five steps, the proof of Claim IV is conducted as follows. By the triangle and Jensen inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\bigg\arrowvert\tr\Big(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad-\tr\bigg[\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad-b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})\tr\Big(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(A^{*}(z_{1})\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\Big[\tr\Big(B^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\Big[\tr\Big(C^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)\Big]\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big\arrowvert\tr\Big(B^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)\Big\arrowvert

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\Big\arrowvert\tr\Big(C^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)\Big\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq K(z_{1},z_{2})\sqrt{m},

where the last inequality is established in steps (i) and (ii). By Jensen’s inequality, the bound
\arrowvert b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})\arrowvert\leq\arrowvert z_{1}\arrowvert/\Im(z_{1})
and steps (iii) and (iv),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\bigg\arrowvert\tr\Big(b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(A^{*}(z_{1})\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)-b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(A_{1}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})\big)\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq K(z_{1},z_{2})\sqrt{m}.

It remains to prove the approximation

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\bigg\arrowvert b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(A_{1}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})\big)+\tr\Big(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.53581pt\times\frac{j-1}{m^{2}}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})b_{1}^{*}(z_{2})\tr\bigg(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big(z_{1}I_{q}-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\bigg)\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq K(z_{1},z_{2})\sqrt{m}.

(E.47)

Applying the approximation of step (v), we deduce

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big\arrowvert b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(A_{1}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})\big)-b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(\tilde{A}_{1}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})\big)\Big\arrowvert\leq K(z_{1},z_{2})\sqrt{m},

with

\displaystyle\tilde{A}_{1}^{*}

\displaystyle(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=-\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{2})\frac{1}{m^{2}}\tr\Big[\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\Big(D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\Big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\times\tr\bigg[D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\bigg].

By Lemma 2.10 of
Bai and Silverstein, (1998)
, we may successively replace
\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{ij}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big)
by
\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big)
within the traces

\displaystyle\bigg\arrowvert\tr\Big[\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\Big(D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\Big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\times\tr\bigg[D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\bigg]

\displaystyle\ \ -\tr\Big[\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\Big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\Big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\times\tr\bigg[D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\bigg]\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq K(z_{1},z_{2})\cdot m,

which proves (
E.47
) and therefore the equation

\displaystyle\tr

\displaystyle\Big(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \times\bigg[1+\frac{j-1}{m^{2}}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})\tr\bigg(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle=-\tr\bigg[\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\bigg]~+\tilde{R}(z_{1},z_{2})

(E.48)

with
\mathbb{E}\arrowvert\tilde{R}(z_{1},z_{2})\arrowvert\leq K\sqrt{m}
. Now, inserting the representation (
E.46
) into (
E.48
), this time for
D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}
, and using (i) and (ii) together with the bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big\arrowvert\tr\big(A(z_{2})M\big)\big\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\not=j}\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\bigg\arrowvert{r_{i}^{*}}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}M\Big(z_{2}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}r_{i}^{*}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ -\frac{1}{m}\tr\Big(D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}M\Big(z_{2}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}\Big)\bigg\arrowvert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq K(z_{2},\Arrowvert M\Arrowvert_{S_{\infty}})\sqrt{m}\Big(1+\frac{m}{\sqrt{n}}\Big)

for non-random matrices
M
of uniformly bounded spectral norm by Proposition
E.1
, Claim IV is verified.

•

Proof of (i).
First, applying the same reasoning as for inequality (2.10) in
Bai and Silverstein, (2004)
, we obtain the spectral norm bound

\displaystyle\Big\Arrowvert\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}\Big\Arrowvert_{S_{\infty}}\leq\frac{1+m/(q\Im(z_{1}))}{\Im(z_{1})}.

(E.49)

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the upper bounds
\arrowvert\beta_{12}^{*}(z)\arrowvert,\arrowvert b_{1}^{*}(z)\arrowvert\leq\arrowvert z\arrowvert/\Im(z)
and
\Arrowvert D_{ij}^{*}(z)^{-1}\Arrowvert_{S_{\infty}}\leq 1/\Im(z)
, (
E.49
),
and Lemma
E.8
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big\arrowvert\tr\big(B^{*}(z_{1})M\big)\big\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq m\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\big\arrowvert\beta_{12}^{*}(z_{1})-b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})\big\arrowvert^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\Big\arrowvert{r_{i}^{*}}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}M\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}r_{i}^{*}\Big\arrowvert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\arrowvert z_{1}\arrowvert^{2}}{(\Im(z_{1}))^{2}}\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\bigg[\big\arrowvert r_{1}^{*\top}r_{1}^{*}\big\arrowvert^{2}\big\Arrowvert D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big\Arrowvert_{S_{\infty}}^{2}\Arrowvert M\Arrowvert_{S_{\infty}}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\times\Big\Arrowvert\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}\Big\Arrowvert_{S_{\infty}}^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq K\frac{\arrowvert z_{1}\arrowvert^{2}(1+m/(q\Im(z_{1})))}{\Im(z_{1})^{5}}\sqrt{m}.

•

Proof of (ii).
By the cyclic invariance of the trace, the bound
\arrowvert b_{1}^{*}(z)\arrowvert\leq\arrowvert z\arrowvert/\Im(z)
, the submultiplicativity of
\Arrowvert\cdot\Arrowvert_{S_{\infty}}
, (
E.49
), and Lemma 2.6 in
Silverstein and Bai, (1995)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big\arrowvert\tr\big(C^{*}(z_{1})M\big)\big\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq m\\
i\not=j\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{m}\big\arrowvert b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})\big\arrowvert\bigg\arrowvert\tr\bigg(\Big(D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}-D_{j}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\Big)M

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\bigg)\bigg\arrowvert\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq Kc\frac{\arrowvert z_{1}\arrowvert(1+m/(q\Im(z_{1})))}{\Im(z_{1})^{3}}.

•

Proof of (iii)
. Because of
\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}=\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\mathbb{E}_{X_{i}^{*}}^{*}
for
i>j
, the triangle and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\tr\Big(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(A^{*}(z_{1})\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)-\tr\Big(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(\check{A}^{*}(z_{1})\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert\sum_{i>j}\tr\Big[\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{X_{i}^{*}}^{*}\Big(r_{i}^{*}{r_{i}^{*}}^{\top}-\frac{1}{m}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 22.76219pt\times\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big]\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i>j}\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\Big\Arrowvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}Y_{i}^{\top}-L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big\Arrowvert_{S_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 22.76219pt\times\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\Big\Arrowvert\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}\Big\Arrowvert_{S_{2}}^{2}.

Applying (
E.49
) and the estimates
\Arrowvert D_{ij}^{*}(z)\Arrowvert_{S_{\infty}}
,
\Arrowvert D_{j}^{*}(z)\Arrowvert_{S_{\infty}}\leq 1/\Im(z)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\Big\Arrowvert\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\times\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}\Big\Arrowvert_{S_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq K(z_{1},z_{2})\sqrt{m},

while

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\Big\Arrowvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}Y_{i}^{\top}-L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big\Arrowvert_{S_{2}}^{2}=\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{m}{\sqrt{n}}\Big),

(E.50)

since both matrices are of dimension
q\times q
.
This proves (iii).

•

Proof of (iv).

Adding and subtracting
D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})
and applying the Sherman-Morrison formula to the difference
D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}-D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}
in the subsequent expression
A_{1}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})
, we obtain the decomposition

\displaystyle\tr\Big(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(\check{A}^{*}(z_{1})\big)

\displaystyle L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)=A_{1}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})+A_{2}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})+A_{3}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})

with

\displaystyle A_{1}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=-\sum_{1\leq i<j}\beta_{ij}^{*}(z_{2}){r_{i}^{*}}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}r_{i}^{*}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times{r_{i}^{*}}^{\top}{r_{i}^{*}}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big(z_{1}I_{q}-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}r_{i}^{*}

\displaystyle A_{2}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=-\tr\bigg\{\sum_{1\leq i<j}\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}\frac{1}{m}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\times\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}-D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}\Big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\bigg\}

\displaystyle A_{3}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=\tr\bigg\{\sum_{1\leq i<j}\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}\Big(r_{i}^{*}{r_{i}^{*}}^{\top}-\frac{1}{m}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\bigg\}.

By Lemma 2.6 in
Silverstein and Bai, (1995)
and (
E.49
),

\displaystyle\big\arrowvert A_{2}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})\big\arrowvert\leq K\frac{1+m/(q\Im(z_{1}))}{(\Im(z_{1}))^{2}}.

Analogously to the proof of (i), we find

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big\arrowvert A_{3}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})\big\arrowvert\leq K\frac{1+m/(q\Im(z_{1}))}{(\Im(z_{1}))^{3}}\sqrt{m}.

•

Proof of (v).
By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and
Lemma
E.8
, we have

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\big(\beta_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})-b_{1}^{*}(z_{2})\big){r_{i}^{*}}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}r_{i}^{*}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times{r_{i}^{*}}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}r_{i}^{*}\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\big\arrowvert\beta_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})-b_{1}^{*}(z_{2})\big\arrowvert^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\bigg\arrowvert{r_{i}^{*}}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}r_{i}^{*}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\ \times{r_{i}^{*}}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}r_{i}^{*}\bigg\arrowvert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq K(z_{1},z_{2})\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}},

where the last inequality follows by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,
(
E.49
), the bound

|{r_{1}^{*}}^{\top}Cr^{*}_{1}|\leq\|C\|_{S_{\infty}}\|r_{1}^{*}\|^{2}
and the fact that
\mathbb{E}\|r_{1}^{*}\|^{p}\leq c
, which follows from (
E.14
).
Next,

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle b_{1}^{*}(z_{2}){r_{i}^{*}}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}r_{i}^{*}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times{r_{i}^{*}}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}r_{i}^{*}

\displaystyle-b_{1}^{*}(z_{2})\frac{1}{m^{2}}\tr\Big[\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\times\tr\bigg[D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\bigg]\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert b_{1}^{*}(z_{2})\frac{1}{m}\tr\Big[\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\bigg({r_{i}^{*}}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}r_{i}^{*}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt-\frac{1}{m}\tr\bigg[D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\bigg]\bigg)\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert b_{1}^{*}(z_{2})\bigg({r_{i}^{*}}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}r_{i}^{*}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-\frac{1}{m}\tr\Big[\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{ij}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big]\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times{r_{i}^{*}}^{\top}D_{ij}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big(z_{1}I-\frac{m-1}{m}b_{1}^{*}(z_{1})L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}r_{i}^{*}\bigg\arrowvert.

By an application of the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to the first term, (
E.49
), the bounds
\arrowvert b_{1}^{*}(z)\arrowvert\leq\arrowvert z\arrowvert/\Im(z)
and
\Arrowvert D_{ij}^{*}(z)^{-1}\Arrowvert_{S_{\infty}}\leq 1/\Im(z)
, and Proposition
E.1
, the right-hand side of the last inequality is bounded by

K(z_{1},z_{2})\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}.

Proof of Claim V.

We shall prove

\big\arrowvert b_{1}^{*}(z)+z\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z)\big\arrowvert=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(m^{-1/2}).

(E.51)

First,

\displaystyle\big\arrowvert b_{1}^{*}(z)-b_{n}^{*}(z)\big\arrowvert

\displaystyle=\Big\arrowvert b_{1}^{*}(z)b_{n}^{*}(z)\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{E}^{*}\tr\Big(L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}-D_{12}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big)\Big)\Big\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\arrowvert z\arrowvert^{2}}{\big(\Im(z)\big)^{3}}\frac{\Arrowvert L_{n}\Arrowvert_{S_{\infty}}^{2}}{m}

by the inequalities
\arrowvert b_{1}^{*}(z)\arrowvert,\arrowvert b_{n}^{*}(z)\arrowvert\leq\arrowvert z\arrowvert/\Im(z)
and Lemma 2.6 in
Silverstein and Bai, (1995)
. Next, note that the estimate
(
B.41
) is still valid under the truncation scheme used in section. This follows by showing the inequality

\mathbb{E}\arrowvert\gamma_{1}^{*}(z)\arrowvert^{2}=\mathcal{O}({1\over m}+\frac{m}{n})

by an application of Proposition
E.1
with
p=2
and using the same arguments following equation (
B.52
). Therefore, it follows that

\mathbb{E}\arrowvert b_{n}^{*}(z)-\mathbb{E}^{*}\beta_{1}^{*}(z)\arrowvert=\mathcal{O}(m^{-1/2}),

and by (
B.40
)
we have

\mathbb{E}^{*}\beta_{1}^{*}(z)=-z\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)
. Together with (
B.29
),
which is also valid under the truncation scheme used in section (see Remark
E.2
),
we conclude (
E.51
). Replacing successively
b_{1}^{*}(z_{i})
in the left-hand side of (
E.43
) by
z_{i}\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z_{i})
,
i=1,2
, and employing Lemma 2.6 in
Silverstein and Bai, (1995)
, the proof of Claim V is completed.

Proof of Claim VI.

Note that we may rewrite (
E.44
) as

\displaystyle{1\over q}\tr\Big(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}

\displaystyle\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}\big(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}\big)L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\times\bigg[1+\frac{j-1}{m^{2}}\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z_{1})\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z_{2})\int\frac{t^{2}}{\big(1+t\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z_{1})\big)\big(1+t\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z_{2})\big)}d\mu^{\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}}(t)\bigg]

\displaystyle={q\over m}\frac{1}{z_{1}z_{2}}\int\frac{t^{2}}{\big(1+t\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z_{1})\big)\big(1+t\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z_{2})\big)}d\mu^{\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}}(t)+\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(m^{1/2}).

As for (2.19) in
Bai and Silverstein, (2004)
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n}\bigg\arrowvert{q\over m}\frac{1}{z_{1}z_{2}}\int\frac{t^{2}}{\big(1+t\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z_{1})\big)\big(1+t\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z_{2})\big)}d\mu^{\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}}(t)\bigg\arrowvert<1.

Denoting

a_{n}(z_{1},z_{2})={q\over m}\frac{1}{z_{1}z_{2}}\int\frac{t^{2}}{\big(1+t\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z_{1})\big)\big(1+t\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z_{2})\big)}d\mu^{\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}}(t),

(
E.40
)
can be written as

a_{n}(z_{1},z_{2})\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Big(1-\frac{j-1}{m}a_{n}(z_{1},z_{2})\Big)^{-1}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(m^{-1/2}).

We now use the Representative Subpopulation Condition
3.1
to
conclude that

a_{n}(z_{1},z_{2})\longrightarrow\frac{c}{z_{1}z_{2}}\int\frac{t^{2}}{\big(1+t\underline{m}^{0}_{c,H}(z_{1})\big)\big(1+t\underline{m}^{0}_{c,H}(z_{2})\big)}dH(t),

(E.52)

in probability. For this purpose, we note that it follows from (
3.1
), (
A.1
) and Condition
3.1

that
\mu^{\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}}\Rightarrow H

in probability and
\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z)\to\underline{m}^{0}_{c,H}(z)
in probability, see equation (
B.32
). Together with the fact that the sequence of bounded continuous functions
