t\mapsto\frac{t^{2}}{\big(1+t\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z_{1})\big)\big(1+t\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z_{2})\big)}

converges in probability uniformly on compacts to its limit, this implies (
E.52
).
The final steps in the proof of (
E.39
) are then the same as on page 578 in
Bai and Silverstein, (2004)
and omitted for the sake of brevity.

E.3.2
Proof of Proposition
D.3

under conditional tightness in probability of
M_{n}^{*}

We start with the following lemma whose assumption can be deduced from the unconditional tightness of the sequence
\mathcal{L}({\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}})

by an application of Markov’s inequality.
Subsequently, we use the abbreviation
U:=(X_{1},,X_{2},\dots\Pi_{1},\Pi_{2},\dots)
and denote by
\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}}(\cdot\,\arrowvert u)
the conditional distribution
\mathcal{L}\big((\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z))_{z\in\mathcal{C}}\arrowvert U=u\big)
.

Lemma E.5
.

Assume that for every
\varepsilon>0
and
\eta>0
, there exists some compact set
K
such that

\sup_{n}\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}}(K^{c}\arrowvert U)>\varepsilon\Big)<\eta.

(E.53)

Then there exists some array of measurable sets
(A_{m,n})_{m,n\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfying
\sup_{n}\mathbb{P}(U\in A_{m,n}^{c})\rightarrow 0
as
m\rightarrow\infty
such that the family

\Big(\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}}(\cdot\arrowvert u):u\in A_{m,n},n\in\mathbb{N}\Big)

is tight for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

Let

\varepsilon\searrow 0
be some null sequence and
\eta_{k}=2^{-k}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
(K_{k})
be some increasing sequence of compacts such that
K_{k}
satisfies (
E.53
) for
\varepsilon_{k}
and
\eta_{k}
. Define

A_{m,n}^{c}:=\bigcup_{k\geq m}\Big\{u:\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}}(K_{k}^{c}\arrowvert u)>\varepsilon_{k}\Big\}.

Every measure
\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}}(\cdot\arrowvert u)
with
u\in A_{m,n}
satisfies
\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}}(K_{k}^{c}\arrowvert u)\leq\varepsilon_{k}
,
k\geq m
. Hence, the family

\Big(\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}}(\cdot\arrowvert u):u\in A_{m,n},n\in\mathbb{N}\Big)

is tight for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
and by the sigma-subadditivity,

\sup_{n}\mathbb{P}(A_{n,m}^{c})\leq\sum_{k\geq m}\frac{1}{2^{k}}\longrightarrow 0\ \ \text{as }m\rightarrow\infty.

∎

Assume now that the condition of Lemma
E.5
holds. Then the weak convergence of the finite dimensional distribution in probability implies
the assertion of Proposition
D.3
, which can be seen as follows.
We define for any measure
R
on the Borel field on the continuous function on
\mathcal{C}
the operation

R\cdot\mathds{1}_{A}(u)=\begin{cases}R&\text{ if }u\in A\\
\delta_{0}&\text{ otherwise. }\end{cases}

Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(d_{\rm BL}

\displaystyle\big(\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}}(\cdot|U),\mathcal{L}(Z)\big)>\varepsilon\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(d_{\rm BL}\big(\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}}(\cdot|U),\mathcal{L}(Z)\big)>\varepsilon,~U\in A_{m.n}\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(U\in A_{m.n}^{c}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\big(d_{\rm BL}\big(\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}}(\cdot|U)\mathds{1}_{A_{m,n}}(U),\mathcal{L}(Z)\mathds{1}_{A_{m,n}}(U)\big)>\varepsilon,~U\in A_{m.n}\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(U\in A_{m.n}^{c}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(d_{\rm BL}\big(\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}}(\cdot|U)\mathds{1}_{A_{m,n}}(U),\mathcal{L}(Z)\mathds{1}_{A_{m,n}}(U)\big)>\varepsilon\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(U\in A_{m.n}^{c}\big)

(E.54)

By Lemma
E.5
, the family
\big\{\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}}(\cdot|u)\mathds{1}_{A_{m,n}}(u)\big\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

is tight uniformly in
u
for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
. The same holds true for the sequence
\big\{\mathcal{L}(Z)\mathds{1}_{A_{m,n}}(u)\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
which attains only the measures
\mathcal{L}(Z)
and
\delta_{0}
.
Now, let
n_{k}
denote an arbitrary subsequence. Then for every
u
, there exists a further subsequence
n_{k}^{\prime}=n_{k}^{\prime}(u)
of
n_{k}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{M}_{n_{k}^{\prime}}^{*}}(\cdot|u)\mathds{1}_{A_{m,n_{k}^{\prime}}}(u)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\nu(u)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(Z)\mathds{1}_{A_{m,n_{k}^{\prime}}}(u)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\bar{\nu}(u)

for some measures
\nu(u)
and
\bar{\nu}(u)
. Moreover, by the weak convergence of the finite dimensional distributions in probability established in Section
E.3.1
, we can choose this subsequence such that additionally,

\mathcal{L}\big((\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z_{1}),\ldots,\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z_{\ell}))|u\big)\Rightarrow\mathcal{L}\big((Z(z_{1}),\ldots,Z(z_{\ell}))|u\big)

for all
z_{1},\ldots,z_{\ell}\in(\mathbb{Q}+i\mathbb{Q}^{+})\cap\mathcal{C}
for
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and for all
u\in A
, where
A
is a measurable set with
\mathbb{P}(A)=1
.
If
\bar{\nu}(u)=\delta_{0}
,
then there exists a
k_{0}=k_{0}(u)
such that
u\in A_{m,n_{k}^{\prime}}^{c}
for all
k\geq k_{0}
. In this case,

d_{\rm BL}\bigg(\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{M}_{n_{k}^{\prime}}^{*}}(\cdot|u)\mathds{1}_{A_{m,n_{k}^{\prime}}}(u),\ \mathcal{L}(Z)\mathds{1}_{A_{m,n_{k}^{\prime}}}(u)\bigg)=0\ \ \text{for all $k\geq k_{0}$.}

Otherwise, if
\bar{\nu}(u)=\mathcal{L}(Z)
, there exists a
k_{1}=k_{1}(u)
such that

u\in A_{m,n_{k}^{\prime}}
for all
k\geq k_{1}
. Consequently it follows that
\nu(u)=\bar{\nu}(u)

which implies

d_{\rm BL}\bigg(\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{M}_{n_{k}^{\prime}}^{*}}(\cdot|u)\mathds{1}_{A_{m,n_{k}^{\prime}}}(u),\ \mathcal{L}(Z)\mathds{1}_{A_{m,n_{k}^{\prime}}}(u)\bigg)\mathds{1}_{A}(u)\rightarrow 0.

Summarizing, we have shown that for any subsequence
(n_{k})
and any
u
, there exists some further subsubsequence
(n_{k}^{\prime}(u))
such that

d_{\rm BL}\bigg(\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{M}_{n_{k}^{\prime}}^{*}}(\cdot|u),\ \mathcal{L}(Z)\bigg)\mathds{1}_{A\cap A_{m,n_{k}^{\prime}(u)}}(u)\rightarrow 0.

Therefore,

d_{\rm BL}\bigg(\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}}(\cdot|u),\ \mathcal{L}(Z)\bigg)\mathds{1}_{A\cap A_{m,n}}(u)\rightarrow 0~~\text{ for every }u~.

By dominated convergence and
\mathbb{P}(A)=1
, this in turn implies

d_{\rm BL}\bigg(\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}}(\cdot|U),\ \mathcal{L}(Z)\bigg)\mathds{1}_{A_{m,n}}(U)\rightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}0.

Thus, it follows from (
E.54
) that

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\big(d_{\rm BL}\big(\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}}(\cdot|U),\mathcal{L}(Z)\big)>\varepsilon\big)\leq\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{P}\big(U\in A_{m.n}^{c}\big)

As the left-hand side does not depend on
m
, the assertion of Proposition
D.3
now follows by taking the limit
m\to\infty
.

E.3.3
Conditional tightness of the process
\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{E}^{*}[\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}]
in probability

It is sufficient to prove the conditional moment condition (12.51) in
Billingsley, (1968)
, which follows from

\displaystyle\sup_{n}\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sup_{z_{1},z_{2}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big[{|\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z_{1})-\mathbb{E}^{*}[\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z_{1})]-(\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z_{2})-\mathbb{E}^{*}[\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z_{2})]|^{2}\over|z_{1}-z_{2}|^{2}}\Big]\geq K\bigg)=o(1)

(E.55)

for
K\to\infty
.
Using similar arguments as in
Bai and Silverstein, (2004)
, p.582 we obtain

\displaystyle{\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z_{1})-\mathbb{E}^{*}[\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z_{1})]-(\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z_{2})-\mathbb{E}^{*}[\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z_{2})])\over z_{1}-z_{2}}

(E.56)

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~=H_{1n}(z_{1},z_{2})+H_{2n}(z_{1},z_{2})+H_{3n}(z_{1},z_{2})

where

\displaystyle H_{1n}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\big(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}-\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\big)\beta_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\beta_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\big(r_{j}{{}^{*}}^{\top}(D_{j}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-1}(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2}))^{-1}r_{j}^{*}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle H_{2n}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j=1}^{m}\big(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}-\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\big)\beta_{j}^{*}(z_{1})r_{j}{{}^{*}}^{\top}(D_{j}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-2}(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2}))^{-1}r_{j}^{*}

\displaystyle H_{3n}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j=1}^{m}\big(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}-\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\big)\beta_{j}^{*}(z_{2})r_{j}{{}^{*}}^{\top}(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2}))^{-2}(D_{j}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-1}r_{j}^{*}

We
begin deriving a uniform conditional moment bound for the quantity
H_{2n}(z_{1},z_{2})
. For this purpose it is crucial to define

\begin{split}\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)&=\Big(1+{1\over m}{\rm tr}(\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big]\big)\Big)^{-1}\\
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)&={r_{1}^{*}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}r_{1}^{*}-{1\over m}{\rm tr}(\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big]\big)~\end{split}

(E.57)

and obtain the identity

\displaystyle\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)=\beta_{1}^{*}(z)+\beta_{1}^{*}(z)\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)

(E.58)

where
\beta_{1}^{*}(z)

is defined in (
A.7
).
Note that for the subsequent arguments, it is essential that the indicator
\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}

is included inside of the conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}^{*}
.
It then follows by a
straightforward calculation that

H_{2n}(z_{1},z_{2})=\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z_{1})W_{1}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})-\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z_{1})W_{2}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})

where

\displaystyle W_{1}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}_{j}-\mathbb{E}^{*}_{j-1}\big)r_{j}{{}^{*}}^{\top}D_{j}^{*}(z_{1})^{-2}D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}r_{j}^{*}~,

\displaystyle W_{2}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}_{j}-\mathbb{E}^{*}_{j-1}\big)\Big[\beta_{j}^{*}(z_{1})r_{j}{{}^{*}}^{\top}D_{j}^{*}(z_{1})^{-2}D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}r_{j}^{*}\tilde{\gamma}_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\Big]~.

Note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{*}|H_{2n}(z_{1},z_{2})|^{2}\leq C\big(|b_{n}^{*}(z_{1})|^{2}\mathbb{E}^{*}|W_{1}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})|^{2}+|b_{n}^{*}(z_{1})|^{2}\mathbb{E}^{*}|W_{2}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})|^{2}\big)~,

(E.59)

and
note that by Lemma
E.11
it follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}|\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)|

(E.60)

is a tight sequence.
We will show at the end of this section that

\displaystyle\sup_{z_{1},z_{2}\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}|W_{j}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})|^{2}~~~\text{are tight sequences ($j=1,2$).}

(E.61)

Therefore, observing (
E.59
) it follows that
\sup_{z_{1},z_{2}\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}|H_{2n}(z_{1},z_{2})|^{2}
is tight as well. The term
H_{3n}(z_{1},z_{2})
in the decomposition (
E.56
) can be treated
in the same way.

To prove tightness of the sequence
\sup_{z_{1},z_{2}\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}|H_{1n}(z_{1},z_{2})|^{2}

we use the same arguments as in
Bai and Silverstein, (2004)
and obtain the representation

\displaystyle H_{1n}(z_{1},z_{2})=\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z_{1})\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z_{2})Y_{1}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})-\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z_{2})Y_{2}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})-\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z_{1})\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z_{2})Y_{3}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2}),

(E.62)

where

\displaystyle Y_{1}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\big(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}-\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\big)\Big[\big(r_{j}{{}^{*}}^{\top}(D_{j}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-1}(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2}))^{-1}r_{j}^{*}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~-\Big({1\over m}{\rm tr}\big(L_{n}(D_{j}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-1}(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2}))^{-1}L_{n}^{\top}\big)\Big)^{2}\Big],

\displaystyle Y_{2}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\big(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}-\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\big)\beta_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\beta_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\big(r_{j}{{}^{*}}^{\top}(D_{j}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-1}(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2}))^{-1}r_{j}^{*}\big)^{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{j}^{*}(z_{2}),

\displaystyle Y_{3}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\big(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}-\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\big)\beta_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\beta_{j}^{*}(z_{2})\big(r_{j}{{}^{*}}^{\top}(D_{j}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-1}(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2}))^{-1}r_{j}^{*}\big)^{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{j}^{*}(z_{1}).

This yields for some constant
C>0

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{*}[|H_{1n}(z_{1},z_{2})|^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq C\big(|\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z_{1})\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z_{2})|^{2}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|Y_{1}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})|^{2}\big]+|\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z_{2})|^{2}\big]\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|Y_{2}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})|^{2}\big]\big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ +|\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z_{1})\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z_{2})|^{2}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|Y_{3}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})|^{2}\big].

(E.63)

At the end of this proof we will show

\displaystyle\sup_{z_{1},z_{2}\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|Y_{j}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})|^{2}\big]~~~\text{ are tight sequences $(j=1,2,3)$.}

(E.64)

It then follows that the sequence
\sup_{z_{1},z_{2}\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}|H_{1n}(z_{1},z_{2})|^{2}

is tight as well and combining this result with (
E.56
)
yields (
E.55
).

Therefore, it remains to show the estimates, (
E.61
) and (
E.64
), which will be done next.

Proof of (
E.61
):
Note that

\displaystyle W_{1}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}_{j}-\mathbb{E}^{*}_{j-1}\big)\Big\{r_{j}{{}^{*}}^{\top}D_{j}^{*}(z_{1})^{-2}D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}r_{j}^{*}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~-{1\over m}\tr\big(L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{j}^{*}(z_{1})^{-2}D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}\big)\Big\}~,

and Proposition
E.3
with the matrix (
E.7
) gives

\displaystyle\sup_{z_{1},z_{2}\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}[|W_{1}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})|^{2}]

\displaystyle\ =\sup_{z_{1},z_{2}\in{\cal C}_{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big[\Big|\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}_{j}-\mathbb{E}^{*}_{j-1}\big)\Big\{r_{j}{{}^{*}}^{\top}D_{j}^{*}(z_{1})^{-2}D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}r_{j}^{*}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~-{1\over m}{\rm tr}\big(L_{n}^{\top}D_{j}^{*}(z_{1})^{-2}D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}\big)\Big)\Big|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\ \leq 4m\sup_{z_{1},z_{2}\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big[\Big|r_{1}{{}^{*}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z_{1})^{-2}D_{1}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}r_{1}^{*}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~-{1\over m}{\rm tr}\big(L_{n}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z_{1})^{-2}D_{1}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}L_{n}\big)\Big|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\ =4mO_{\mathbb{P}}\Big({1\over m}+{m\over n}\Big)=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(E.65)

As concerns the term
W_{2}^{*}

(z_{1},z_{2})
, we find

\displaystyle\sup_{z_{1},z_{2}\in{\cal C}_{n}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{*}[|W_{2}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})|^{2}]

\displaystyle=\sup_{z_{1},z_{2}\in{\cal C}_{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big[\Big|\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}_{j}-\mathbb{E}^{*}_{j-1}\big)\big[\beta_{j}^{*}(z_{1})r_{j}{{}^{*}}^{\top}D_{j}^{*}(z_{1})^{-2}D_{j}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}r_{j}^{*}\tilde{\gamma}_{j}^{*}(z_{1})\big]\Big|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\ \leq 4m\sup_{z_{1},z_{2}\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big|\beta_{1}^{*}(z_{1})r_{1}{{}^{*}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z_{1})^{-2}D_{1}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}r_{1}^{*}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z_{1})\big|^{2}~.

Note that on the set
{\mathcal{A}_{n}}
,

\displaystyle\|r_{1}^{*}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\|r_{1}^{*}{r_{1}^{*}}^{\top}\|_{S_{\infty}}\leq\Big\|\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}^{*}{r_{j}^{*}}^{\top}\Big\|_{S_{\infty}}+\Big\|\sum_{j=2}^{m}r_{j}^{*}{r_{j}^{*}}^{\top}\Big\|_{S_{\infty}}\leq 2K_{\rm right},

(E.66)

and, recalling the definition of
D^{*}
in (
A.3
) and (
E.13
), yields on
{\cal A}_{n}

|\beta_{1}^{*}(z)|=|1-{r_{j}^{*}}^{\top}D^{*}(z)^{-1}{r_{j}^{*}}|\leq 1+\|D^{*}(z)^{-1}\|_{S_{\infty}}\|{r_{j}^{*}}\|^{2}<c.

(E.67)

We now use the decomposition
\mathds{1}=\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}+\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}^{c}}
and (
E.10
)
to obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{z_{1},z_{2}\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}[|W_{2}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})|^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq 4m\sup_{z_{1}\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big|\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z_{1})\big|^{2}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Recalling the definition of
\varepsilon_{1}^{*}(z)
in (
A.5
), Burkholder’s inequality, we obtain the bound (for any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
)

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)|^{2}\big]\lesssim\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|\gamma_{1}^{*}(z)-\varepsilon_{1}^{*}(z)|^{2}\big]+\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|\varepsilon^{*}_{1}(z)|^{2}\big]

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\Big|{1\over m}{\rm tr}(\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}^{c}}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big]\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}{1\over m^{2}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big|\sum_{j=2}^{m}(\mathbb{E}^{*}_{j}-\mathbb{E}^{*}_{j-1})\beta_{1j}^{*}(z){r_{j}^{*}}^{\top}D_{1j}^{*}(z)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D_{1j}^{*}(z)^{-1}{r_{j}^{*}}\Big|^{2}+\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|\varepsilon^{*}_{1}(z)|^{2}\big]

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+O_{\mathbb{P}}\big(m^{2(1+\alpha)-\ell}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim{1\over m}\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[\big|\beta_{12}^{*}(z){r_{2}^{*}}^{\top}D_{12}^{*}(z)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D_{12}^{*}(z)^{-1}{r_{2}^{*}}\big|^{2}\big]+\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|\varepsilon^{*}_{1}(z)|^{2}\big]

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+O_{\mathbb{P}}\big(m^{2(1+\alpha)-\ell}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim{1\over m}\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[\big|\beta_{12}^{*}(z){r_{2}^{*}}^{\top}D_{12}^{*}(z)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D_{12}^{*}(z)^{-1}{r_{2}^{*}}\big|^{2}\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}\big]+\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|\varepsilon^{*}_{1}(z)|^{2}\big]

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+O_{\mathbb{P}}\big(m^{2(1+\alpha)-\ell}\big)

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}\Big({1\over m}\Big)+O_{\mathbb{P}}\big(m^{2(1+\alpha)-\ell}\big)+\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|\varepsilon^{*}_{1}(z)|^{2}\big],

where
\beta_{12}^{*}(z)
is defined in (
A.11
)
and is bounded on the set
{\mathcal{A}_{n}}
,
which follows by similar calculations as used for the derivation of (
E.67
)). Moreover, by Proposition
E.3
, we have

\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|\varepsilon^{*}_{1}(z)|^{2}\big]=O_{\mathbb{P}}\Big({1\over m}+\frac{m}{n}\Big),

which gives

\displaystyle m\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)|^{2}\big]=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)+O_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\frac{m^{2}}{n}\Big)=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

(E.68)

and proves (
E.61
).

Proof of (
E.64
):
For the sake of brevity, we
restrict ourselves to the term
Y_{1}^{*}
. Corresponding results for
Y_{2}^{*}
and
Y_{3}^{*}
are derived in a similar way.
Note that

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}

\displaystyle|Y_{1}^{*}(z_{1},z_{2})|^{2}=\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big|\big(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}-\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*}\big)\Big[\big(r_{j}{{}^{*}}^{\top}(D_{j}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-1}(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2}))^{-1}r_{j}^{*}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~-\Big({1\over m}{\rm tr}\big(L_{n}(D_{j}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-1}(D_{j}^{*}(z_{2}))^{-1}L_{n}^{\top}\big)\Big)^{2}\Big]\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4m\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big|\big(r_{1}{{}^{*}}^{\top}(D_{1}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-1}(D_{1}^{*}(z_{2}))^{-1}r_{1}^{*}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt-\big({1\over m}{\rm tr}\big(L_{n}(D_{1}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-1}(D_{1}^{*}(z_{2}))^{-1}L_{n}^{\top}\big)\big)^{2}\Big|^{2}

We recall the definition of the set
\mathcal{A}_{n}
defined in (
E.9
), the decomposition

\mathds{1}=\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}+\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}^{c}}

and the identity

|a^{2}-b^{2}|^{2}=|a-b|^{4}+2(\bar{a}b+a\bar{b})|a-b|^{2}
, which give (observing (
E.10
))

\displaystyle m\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}^{c}}

\displaystyle\Big|\big(r_{1}{{}^{*}}^{\top}(D_{1}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-1}(D_{1}^{*}(z_{2}))^{-1}r_{1}^{*}\big)^{2}-\big({1\over m}{\rm tr}\big(L_{n}(D_{1}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-1}(D_{1}^{*}(z_{2}))^{-1}L_{n}^{\top}\big)\big)^{2}\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

and

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}\Big|\big(r_{1}{{}^{*}}^{\top}(D_{1}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-1}(D_{1}^{*}(z_{2}))^{-1}r_{1}^{*}\big)^{2}-\big({1\over m}{\rm tr}\big(L_{n}(D_{1}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-1}(D_{1}^{*}(z_{2}))^{-1}L_{n}^{\top}\big)\big)^{2}\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big|r_{1}{{}^{*}}^{\top}(D_{1}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-1}(D_{1}^{*}(z_{2}))^{-1}r_{1}^{*}-{1\over m}{\rm tr}\big(L_{n}(D_{1}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-1}(D_{1}^{*}(z_{2}))^{-1}L_{n}^{\top}\big)\Big|^{4}

\displaystyle+4\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big[\Big|r_{1}{{}^{*}}^{\top}(D_{1}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-1}(D_{1}^{*}(z_{2}))^{-1}r_{1}^{*}-{1\over m}{\rm tr}\big(L_{n}(D_{1}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-1}(D_{1}^{*}(z_{2}))^{-1}L_{n}^{\top}\big)\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\times\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}\|(D_{1}^{*}(z_{1}))^{-1}(D_{1}^{*}(z_{2}))^{-1}\|_{{S}_{\infty}}^{2}{q\over m}\|r_{1}^{*}\|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}\Big({1\over m}\Big),

where we have used Proposition
E.3
for the matrix (
E.8
) and the estimate (
E.13
) on the set
\mathcal{A}_{n}
. The assertion (
E.64
) for
Y_{1}^{*}
now follows.

E.3.4
Auxiliary results for the proof of Proposition
D.3

Recall that in the next two Lemmas, we consider
truncated, centered and normalized
q^{\prime}
-dimensional random vector
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}

(see the discussion in Section
D.1
and
D.2
).

Lemma E.6
.

For
k=1,\ldots,m
let

\displaystyle A_{k}(z)=L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{k-1}^{*}\big(\bar{\beta}_{k}^{*}(z)D_{k}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big)L_{n}\ .

Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert\mathbb{E}_{X_{k}^{*}}^{*}\Big(X_{k}^{*\top}A_{k}(z_{1})X_{k}^{*}-\tr(A_{k}(z_{1}))\Big)\mathbb{E}_{X_{k}^{*}}^{*}\Big(X_{k}^{*\top}A_{k}(z_{2})X_{k}^{*}-\tr(A_{k}(z_{2}))\Big)\bigg\arrowvert

(E.69)

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\leq K(z_{1},z_{2})\Big({\delta_{m}^{2}m^{2}\over\sqrt{n}}+\frac{m^{3}}{n}\Big)

Evaluating
\mathbb{E}_{X_{k}^{*}}^{*}
provides the upper bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big\arrowvert

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{k}^{*}}^{*}\Big(X_{k}^{*\top}A_{k}(z_{1})X_{k}^{*}-\tr(A_{k}(z_{1}))\Big)\mathbb{E}_{X_{k}^{*}}^{*}\Big(X_{k}^{*\top}A_{k}(z_{2})X_{k}^{*}-\tr(A_{k}(z_{2}))\Big)\Big\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{1})X_{i}-\tr(A_{k}(z_{1}))\big)\big(X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{2})X_{i}-\tr(A_{k}(z_{2}))\big)\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,i^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots n\}:\\
i\not=i^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\big(X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{1})X_{i}-\tr(A_{k}(z_{1}))\big)\big(X_{i^{\prime}}\top A_{k}(z_{2})X_{i^{\prime}}-\tr(A_{k}(z_{2}))\big)\bigg\arrowvert.

(E.70)

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{1})X_{i}-\tr(A_{k}(z_{1}))\big)\big(X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{2})X_{i}-\tr(A_{k}(z_{2}))\big)\bigg\arrowvert

(E.71)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\big\arrowvert X_{1}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{1})X_{1}-\tr(A_{k}(z_{1}))\big\arrowvert^{2}\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\big\arrowvert X_{1}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{2})X_{1}-\tr(A_{k}(z_{2}))\big\arrowvert.

We now investigate one factor using Jensen’s inequality for the conditional expectation and the bound

|\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z)|\leq\frac{\arrowvert z\arrowvert}{\Im(z)}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big\arrowvert

\displaystyle X_{1}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{1})X_{1}-\tr(A_{k}(z_{1}))\big\arrowvert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{\arrowvert z_{1}\arrowvert}{\Im(z_{1})}\Big)^{2}\mathbb{E}\big\arrowvert X_{1}^{\top}L_{n}^{\top}D_{k}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}(z_{1})L_{n}X_{1}-\tr(L_{n}^{\top}D_{k}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}L_{n})\big\arrowvert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq K\Big(m+{m^{3}\over n}\Big),

where last inequality follows by the same arguments as given after formula (
E.11
) in the proof Proposition
E.1
. Therefore, the right-hand side of (
E.71
) is bounded by

K\frac{\arrowvert z_{1}\arrowvert\arrowvert z_{2}\arrowvert}{\Im(z_{1})\Im(z_{2})}\Big(1+\frac{m^{2}}{n}\Big)\frac{m}{n}.

We define
i^{*}_{\ell}
as the index
j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
with
X_{\ell}^{*}=X_{j}

For
k=1,\ldots,m
, we denote
\hat{I}_{k}=\{1,\dots,n\}\cap\{i_{1}^{*},\ldots,i_{k-1}^{*},i_{k+1}^{*},\ldots,i_{m}^{*}\}
and observe that
\sharp\hat{I}_{k}\leq m
and that
\hat{I}_{k},X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
are jointly independent. With the notation

R_{ki}(z)=X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z)X_{i}-\tr(A_{k}(z)),\ \ i=1,\dots,n,µk=1,\ldots,m,

we decompose the expression in (
E.70
) into

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,i^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots n\}:\\
i\not=i^{\prime}\end{subarray}}R_{ki}(z_{1})R_{ki^{\prime}}(z_{2})\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,i^{\prime}\in\hat{I}_{k},i\not=i^{\prime}}R_{ki}(z_{1})R_{ki^{\prime}}(z_{2})\bigg\arrowvert+\ \mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,i^{\prime}\in\hat{I}_{k}^{c},i\not=i^{\prime}}R_{ki}(z_{1})R_{ki^{\prime}}(z_{2})\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}_{k}^{c},i^{\prime}\in\hat{I}_{k}}R_{ki}(z_{1})R_{ki^{\prime}}(z_{2})\bigg\arrowvert+\mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}_{k},i^{\prime}\in\hat{I}_{k}^{c}}R_{ki}(z_{1})R_{ki^{\prime}}(z_{2})\bigg\arrowvert

and bound each summand separately.
By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,i^{\prime}\in\hat{I}_{k},i\not=i^{\prime}}R_{1i}R_{2i^{\prime}}\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\not=i^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}^{1/4}(I{\{i\in\hat{I}_{k}\}})\mathbb{E}^{1/4}(I{\{i^{\prime}\in\hat{I}_{k}\}})\mathbb{E}^{1/4}\arrowvert R_{1i}\arrowvert^{4}\mathbb{E}^{1/4}\arrowvert R_{2i^{\prime}}\arrowvert^{4}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{K}\Big({\delta_{m}^{2}m^{2}\over\sqrt{n}}+\frac{m^{3}}{n}\Big).

For the last inequality, we use Jensen’s inequality for the conditional expectation, the same arguments as given after formula (
E.11
) in the proof Proposition
E.1
and the fact that
\mathbb{E}(I{\{i\in\hat{I}_{k}\}})\leq{m\over n}
, which follows because
\mathbb{E}(I{\{i\in\hat{I}_{k}\}})
is independent of
i
and
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}(I{\{i\in\hat{I}_{k}\}})\leq m
.

Observing that, by Jensen’s inequality, the conditional
expectations

\mathbb{E}[R_{ki}(z_{1})\arrowvert\hat{I}_{k},\{X_{j}:j\in\hat{I}_{k}\}]

vanish for
i\in\hat{I}^{c}_{k}
, the
conditional independence of
R_{ki(z)}
and
R_{ki^{\prime}}(z)
for
i\not=i^{\prime}
,
i,i^{\prime}\in\hat{I}^{c}_{k}
given
\hat{I}_{k}
and
\{X_{j}:j\in\hat{I}_{k}\}
, and Lemma 2.7 of
Bai and Silverstein, (1998)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{2}

\displaystyle\bigg\arrowvert\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,i^{\prime}\in\hat{I}_{k}^{c},i\not=i^{\prime}}R_{ki}(z_{1})R_{ki^{\prime}}(z_{2})\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{i,i^{\prime}\in\hat{I}_{k}^{c},i\not=i^{\prime}}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\Big[R_{ki}(z_{1})\overline{R}_{ki}(z_{1})R_{ki^{\prime}}(z_{2})\overline{R}_{ki^{\prime}}(z_{2})\Big\arrowvert\hat{I}_{k},\{X_{j}:j\in\hat{I}_{k}\}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\mathbb{E}\Big[R_{ki}(z_{1})R_{ki}(z_{2})\overline{R}_{ki^{\prime}}(z_{1})\overline{R}_{ki^{\prime}}(z_{2})\Big\arrowvert\hat{I}_{k},\{X_{j}:j\in\hat{I}_{k}\}\Big]\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{i,i^{\prime}\in\hat{I}k^{c},i\not=i^{\prime}}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\big[\arrowvert R_{ki}(z_{1})\arrowvert^{2}\big\arrowvert\hat{I}_{k},\{X_{j}:j\in\hat{I}_{k}\}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[\arrowvert R_{ki^{\prime}}(z_{2})\arrowvert^{2}\big\arrowvert\hat{I}_{k},\{X_{j}:j\in\hat{I}_{k}\}\big]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\mathbb{E}\big[R_{ki}(z_{1})R_{ki}(z_{2})\big\arrowvert\hat{I}_{k},\{X_{j}:j\in\hat{I}_{k}\}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[\overline{R}_{ki^{\prime}}(z_{1})\overline{R}_{2i^{\prime}}(z_{2})\big\arrowvert\hat{I}_{k},\{X_{j}:j\in\hat{I}_{k}\}\big]\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq K\frac{m^{2}}{n^{2}}.

Next, by the same arguments as above and the same arguments as given after formula (
E.11
) in the proof of Proposition
E.1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{2}\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}_{k}^{c},i^{\prime}\in\hat{I}_{k}}R_{ki}(z_{1})R_{ki^{\prime}}(z_{2})\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,l\in\hat{I}_{k}^{c}\\
i^{\prime},l^{\prime}\in\hat{I}_{k}\end{subarray}}R_{ki^{\prime}}(z_{2})\overline{R}_{kl^{\prime}}(z_{2})\mathbb{E}\Big[R_{ki}(z_{1})\overline{R}_{kl}(z_{1})\Big\arrowvert\hat{I}_{k},\{X_{j}:j\in\hat{I}_{k}\}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\hat{I}_{k}^{c}\\
i^{\prime},l^{\prime}\in\hat{I}_{k}\end{subarray}}R_{ki^{\prime}}(z_{2})\overline{R}_{kl^{\prime}}(z_{2})\mathbb{E}\Big[R_{ki}(z_{1})\overline{R}_{ki}(z_{1})\Big\arrowvert\hat{I}_{k},\{X_{j}:j\in\hat{I}_{k}\}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq K(z_{1})\frac{m}{n^{3}}\sum_{i,i^{\prime},i\not=i^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\arrowvert R_{ki^{\prime}}(z_{2})\arrowvert^{2}\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\arrowvert R_{kl^{\prime}}(z_{2})\arrowvert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq K(z_{1},z_{2})\frac{m^{2}}{n}.

Here, we applied Lemma B.26 of
Bai and Silverstein, (2010)
to obtain

\mathbb{E}\Big[R_{ki}(z_{1})\overline{R}_{ki}(z_{1})\Big\arrowvert\hat{I}_{k},\{X_{j}:j\in\hat{I}_{k}\}\Big]\leq K(z_{1})m,

which yields the second inequality.
The last expression

\mathbb{E}\bigg\arrowvert\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}_{k},i^{\prime}\in\hat{I}_{k}^{c}}R_{ki}(z_{1})R_{ki^{\prime}}(z_{2})\bigg\arrowvert

in the decomposition of (
E.70
) can be bounded analogously. Summarizing these calculations yield (
E.69
), which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma E.7
.

For any
k=1,\ldots,m
define

\displaystyle A_{k}(z)=\frac{1}{m}L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{k-1}^{*}\big(D_{k}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big)L_{n}=\frac{1}{m}L_{n}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{k}^{*}\big(D_{k}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big)L_{n}~.

Then there exists some positive constant
K(z_{1},z_{2})
(independent of
k
) such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\Big\arrowvert\mathbb{E}_{X_{k}^{*}}\Big[\big(X_{k}^{*\top}A_{k}(z_{1})X_{k}^{*}-\tr A_{k}(z_{1})\big)\big(X_{k}^{*\top}A_{k}(z_{2})X_{k}^{*}-\tr A_{k}(z_{2})\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt-2\tr(A_{k}(z_{1})A_{k}(z_{2}))\Big]\Big\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq K(z_{1},z_{2})\Big(\frac{\delta_{m}^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{m}{n}+o\Big(\frac{1}{m}\Big)\Big).

Recall the notation of
\hat{I}_{k}
in the proof of Lemma
E.6
.
Evaluating
\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}^{*}}^{*}
provides the upper bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\Big\arrowvert\mathbb{E}_{X_{k}^{*}}\Big[\big(X_{k}^{*\top}A_{k}(z_{1})X_{k}^{*}-\tr A_{k}(z_{1})\big)\big(X_{k}^{*\top}A_{k}(z_{2})X_{k}^{*}-\tr A_{k}(z_{2})\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt-2\tr(A_{k}(z_{1})A_{k}(z_{2}))\Big]\Big\arrowvert

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big\arrowvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{1})X_{i}-\tr A_{k}(z_{1})\big)\big(X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{2})X_{i}-\tr A_{k}(z_{2})\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt-2\tr(A_{k}(z_{1})A_{k}(z_{2}))\Big\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big\arrowvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}_{k}}\big(X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{1})X_{i}-\tr A_{k}(z_{1})\big)\big(X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{2})X_{i}-\tr A_{k}(z_{2})\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt-2\tr(A_{k}(z_{1})A_{k}(z_{2}))\Big\arrowvert

(E.72)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\mathbb{E}\Big\arrowvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}_{k}^{c}}\big(X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{1})X_{i}-\tr A_{k}(z_{1})\big)\big(X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{2})X_{i}-\tr A_{k}(z_{2})\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt-2\tr(A_{k}(z_{1})A_{k}(z_{2}))\Big\arrowvert

(E.73)

We start with bounding (
E.72
). Since
\arrowvert\tr(A_{k}(z_{1})A_{k}(z_{2}))\arrowvert\leq m\|A_{k}(z_{1})\|_{S_{\infty}}\|A_{k}(z_{2})\|_{S_{\infty}}
,
\|A_{k}(z_{1})\|_{S_{\infty}}\leq K(z_{1})/m
and
\sharp\hat{I}_{k}\leq m
, we have

\mathbb{E}\Big\arrowvert\frac{\sharp\hat{I}_{k}}{n}\tr(A_{1}A_{2})\Big\arrowvert\leq K(z_{1},z_{2})\frac{1}{n}.

Next, by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and
by the same arguments as given after formula (
E.11
) in the proof Proposition
E.1
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\Big\arrowvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}_{k}}\big(X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{1})X_{i}-\tr A_{k}(z_{1})\big)\big(X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{2})X_{i}-\tr A_{k}(z_{2})\big)\Big\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\big(\mathds{1}_{\{i\in\hat{I}_{k}\}}\big)\mathbb{E}^{1/4}\big\arrowvert X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{1})X_{i}-\tr A_{k}(z_{1})\big\arrowvert^{4}

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt\times\mathbb{E}^{1/4}\big\arrowvert X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{2})X_{i}-\tr A_{k}(z_{2})\big\arrowvert^{4}

\displaystyle\leq K(z_{1},z_{2})\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Big(\delta_{m}^{2}+\frac{m}{\sqrt{n}}\Big).

As concerns (
E.73
),
we use the same arguments as in the proof of Lemma
E.6
and obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{2}\bigg\arrowvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}_{k}^{c}}\big(X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{1})X_{i}-\tr A_{k}(z_{1})\big)\big(X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{2})X_{i}-\tr A_{k}(z_{2})\big)-2\tr(A_{k}(z_{1})A_{k}(z_{2}))\bigg\arrowvert

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,i^{\prime}\in\hat{I}_{k}^{c}}\Big[\big(X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{1})X_{i}-\tr A_{k}(z_{1})\big)\big(X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{2})X_{i}-\tr A_{k}(z_{2})\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 256.0748pt-2\tr(A_{k}(z_{1})A_{k}(z_{2}))\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times\Big[\big(X_{i^{\prime}}^{\top}\overline{A_{k}(z_{1})}X_{i^{\prime}}-\tr\overline{A_{k}(z_{1})}\big)\big(X_{i^{\prime}}^{\top}\overline{A_{k}(z_{2})}X_{i^{\prime}}-\tr\overline{A_{k}(z_{2})}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 256.0748pt-2\tr(\overline{A_{k}(z_{1})A_{k}(z_{2})})\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 304.44447pt\bigg\arrowvert\hat{I}_{k},\{X_{j}:j\in\hat{I}_{k}\}\bigg]\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}_{k}^{c}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big\arrowvert\big(X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{1})X_{i}-\tr A_{k}(z_{1})\big)\big(X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{2})X_{i}-\tr A_{k}(z_{2})\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-2\tr(A_{l}(z_{1})A_{k}(z_{2}))\Big\arrowvert^{2}\bigg\arrowvert\hat{I}_{k},\{X_{j}:j\in\hat{I}_{k}\}\bigg]~+~K(z_{1},z_{2})o\Big(\frac{1}{m^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\Big[\big\arrowvert X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{1})X_{i}-\tr A_{k}(z_{1})\big\arrowvert^{4}\Big\arrowvert\hat{I}_{k},\{X_{j}:j\in\hat{I}_{k}\}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\Big[\big\arrowvert\big\arrowvert X_{i}^{\top}A_{k}(z_{2})X_{i}-\tr A_{k}(z_{2})\big\arrowvert^{4}\Big\arrowvert\hat{I}_{k},\{X_{j}:j\in\hat{I}_{k}\}\Big]\bigg\}

\displaystyle\hskip 187.78818pt+\frac{8}{n}\mathbb{E}\arrowvert\tr(A_{k}(z_{1})A_{k}(z_{2}))\arrowvert^{2}~+~K(z_{1},z_{2})o\Big(\frac{1}{m^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq K(z_{1},z_{2})\Big(\frac{\delta_{m}^{4}}{mn}+\frac{m}{n^{2}}+o\Big(\frac{1}{m^{2}}\Big)\Big),

where the last line follows from Lemma B.26
in
Bai and Silverstein, (2010)
and the truncation at level
\delta_{m}\sqrt{m}
. In this estimate, the term
K(z_{1},z_{2})o(1/m^{2})
is caused by the sum over the mixed products with indices
i\not=i^{\prime}
where the conditional expectation factorizes. Its bound is due to (
D.7
) and formula (1.15) in
Bai and Silverstein, (2004)
, using that

\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{q^{\prime}}(A_{k}(z_{1}))_{ii}(A_{k}(z_{2}))_{ii}\Big|\leq\|A_{k}(z_{1})\|_{S_{2}}\|A_{k}(z_{2})\|_{S_{2}}\leq\frac{K(z_{1},z_{2})}{m}.

∎

Lemma E.8
.

There exists some constant
K(z)>0
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\arrowvert\bar{\beta}_{1}^{*}(z)-b_{n}^{*}(z)\arrowvert^{2}\leq K(z)\frac{1}{m}~,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\arrowvert{\beta}_{12}^{*}(z)-b_{1}^{*}(z)\arrowvert^{2}\leq K(z)\frac{1}{m}~.

Note that by Proposition
E.1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big|\bar{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z)-{\beta}_{j}^{*}(z)\big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg|\frac{r_{j}^{*^{\top}}{D_{j}^{*}}(z)^{-1}r_{j}^{*}-{1\over m}\tr\big(L_{n}L_{n}^{{}^{\top}}{D_{j}^{*}}(z)^{-1})}{(1+r_{j}^{*^{\top}}{D_{j}^{*}}(z)^{-1}r_{j}^{*})(1+{1\over m}\tr\big(L_{n}L_{n}^{{}^{\top}}{D_{j}^{*}}(z)^{-1}\big)}\bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big({|z|\over\Im(z)}\Big)^{2}\mathbb{E}\Big|{r_{j}^{*^{\top}}{D_{j}^{*}}(z)^{-1}r_{j}^{*}-{1\over m}\tr\big(L_{n}L_{n}^{{}^{\top}}{D_{j}^{*}}(z)^{-1})}\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq K(z)\Big({1\over m}+{m\over n}\Big)\leq K(z){1\over m}~.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\arrowvert{\beta}_{1}^{*}(z)-b_{n}^{*}(z)\arrowvert^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}|{\beta}_{1}^{*}(z)b_{n}^{*}(z))\gamma_{1}^{*}(z)|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big({|z|\over\Im(z)}\Big)^{2}\mathbb{E}|\gamma_{1}^{*}(z)|^{2}\leq K(z)\Big({1\over m}+{m\over n}\Big)\leq K(z){1\over m},

which follows as in (
B.52
), where use Proposition
E.1
instead of Proposition
B.1
because of the different truncation scheme. The first assertion now follows by Minkowskii’s inequality. The second inequality is obtained analogously.
∎

E.4
Proof of Proposition
D.4

Note that
M_{n}^{*}
and
\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}
coincide on
{\cal C}_{n}
. By the same calculations as on pages 588-589
Bai and Silverstein, (2004)
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z)\big]

\displaystyle=q(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)-\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z))

\displaystyle=-\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z){q\over m}mA_{n}^{*}(z)\Big[1-{q\over m}\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z)\int\frac{t^{2}\,d\mu^{\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}}(t)}{(1+t\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z))(1+t\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z))}\Big]^{-1},

(E.74)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}A_{n}^{*}(z)&={q\over m}\int\frac{d\mu^{\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}}(t)}{1+t\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)}-{q\over m}+z\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)(z)+1\\
&=-\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\left(-z-\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)}+{q\over m}\int\frac{t\,d\mu^{\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}}(t)}{1+t\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)}\right).\end{split}

(E.75)

Recall that
\tilde{\underline{m}}^{0}_{n}=\underline{m}_{p/n,\mu^{\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}}}^{0}(z)
is the solution of the Marc̆enko-Pastur equation (
2.5
) for
\mu^{\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}}
.
As

\mu^{\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}}\Rightarrow H

and
{\cal C}
lies outside the compact support of
H
, the sequence
\tilde{\underline{m}}^{0}_{n}
converges uniformly on
{\cal C}_{n}
to
\underline{m}_{H}^{0}
and by Lemma
E.9
, the sequence

(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

has the same uniform limit in probability.
Therefore we obtain by Lemmas
E.9
,
E.10
and the same arguments as given on page 585 in
Bai and Silverstein, (2004)
that the sequence

1-{q\over m}\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z)\int\frac{t^{2}\,d\mu^{\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}}(t)}{(1+t\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z))(1+t\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z))}

is uniformly bounded away from
0
in probability and its inverse converges uniformly to

\displaystyle\Big[1-c\int\frac{(\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0}(z))^{2}t^{2}\,d{H}(t)}{(1+t\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0}(z))^{2}}\Big]^{-1}.

(E.76)

Hence it is sufficient to prove uniform convergence of the sequence
(qA_{n}^{*}(z))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
. To this aim we note that we obtain from (
B.34
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}mA_{n}^{*}(z)=&m\Big({q\over m}\int{d\mu^{\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}}(t)\over 1+t\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}^{*}_{n}(z)}+z{q\over m}\mathbb{E}^{*}{m}^{*}_{n}(z)\Big)\\
&=m\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg\{\beta_{1}^{*}(z)\Big[r_{1}^{\ast}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}r_{1}^{*}\\
&-\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{E}^{*}\operatorname{tr}\big\{\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D^{*}(z)^{-1}\big\}\Big]\bigg\}\end{split}~.

(E.77)

Recalling the definition of

\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)
,
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)
in (
E.57
) and the identity (
E.58
)
we now investigate the difference

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[\operatorname{tr}\big\{\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big\}\big]-\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[\operatorname{tr}\big\{\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D^{*}(z)^{-1}\big\}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[\beta_{1}^{*}(z){\rm tr}\big\{\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}r_{1}^{*}{r_{1}^{*}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big\}\big]

\displaystyle=\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[(1-\beta_{1}^{*}(z)\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)){r_{1}^{*}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}r_{1}^{*}\big]

where we used the Sherman-Morrison formula and (
E.58
) for the last identity.
By Lemma
E.10
, Lemma
E.11
, (
E.66
) and (
E.67
) and the fact that the spectral norm of
D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}
is uniformly bounded on
{\cal A}_{n}
it follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}\beta_{1}^{*}(z)\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z){r_{1}^{*}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}r_{1}^{*}\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\|\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\|_{S_{\infty}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}|\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)|\big]

\displaystyle=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

Similarly, on
{\cal A}_{n}^{c}
we use the estimates

\|r_{1}^{*}\|^{2}\leq\delta_{n}^{2}m~,~~\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\|D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\|_{S_{\infty}}\lesssim{m^{1+\alpha}}~,~~\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}|\beta_{1}^{*}(z)|\lesssim{\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}|z|\over|\Im(z)|}\lesssim{m^{1+\alpha}},

and obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}^{*}\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}^{c}}\big[\beta_{1}^{*}(z)\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z){r_{1}^{*}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}r_{1}^{*}\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\Big\|\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\Big\|_{S_{\infty}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}^{c}}\big]{m^{10+4\alpha}}\delta_{n}^{4}

\displaystyle=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

Consequently, it follows from (
E.97
) in Lemma
E.11
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{*}

\displaystyle\big[\operatorname{tr}\big\{\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big\}\big]

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~-\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[\operatorname{tr}\big\{\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D^{*}(z)^{-1}\big\}\big]

(E.78)

\displaystyle=\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[{r_{1}^{*}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}r_{1}^{*}\big]

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ +o_{\mathbb{P},{\rm unif}}(1)~,

where
X_{n}(z)=o_{\mathbb{P},{\rm unif}}(1)
means that
\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}|X_{n}(z)|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Using this approximation in (
E.77
) yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}mA_{n}^{*}(z)&=m\Big({q\over m}\int{d\mu^{\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}}(t)\over 1+\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}^{*}_{n}(z)}+z{q\over m}\mathbb{E}^{*}{m}^{*}_{n}(z)\Big)\\
&=m\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg\{\beta_{1}^{*}(z)\Big[r_{1}^{\ast}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}r_{1}^{*}\\
&-\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{E}^{*}\operatorname{tr}\big\{\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D^{*}_{1}(z)^{-1}\big\}\Big]\\
&+\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)\mathbb{E}^{*}[\beta_{1}^{*}(z)]\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[{r_{1}^{*}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}r_{1}^{*}\big]\bigg\}+o_{\mathbb{P},{\rm unif}}(1)~.\end{split}

(E.79)

Replacing
\beta_{1}^{*}(z)=\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)-\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)^{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)+\beta_{1}^{*}(z)\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)^{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)^{2}
, where
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)
and
\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)
are defined in (
E.57
), we get for the first part

\displaystyle m\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg\{\beta_{1}^{*}(z)\Big[r_{1}^{\ast}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}r_{1}^{*}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~-\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{E}^{*}\operatorname{tr}\big\{\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D^{*}_{1}(z)^{-1}\big\}\Big]\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)m\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big[r_{1}^{\ast}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}r_{1}^{*}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~-\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{E}^{*}\operatorname{tr}\big\{\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D^{*}_{1}(z)^{-1}\big\}\Big]

\displaystyle-m\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)^{2}\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big\{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)\Big[{r_{1}^{\ast}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}r_{1}^{*}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~-\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{E}^{*}\operatorname{tr}\big\{\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D^{*}_{1}(z)^{-1}\big\}\Big]\Big\}

\displaystyle+\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)^{2}m\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg\{\beta_{1}^{*}(z)\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)^{2}\bigg[{r_{1}^{\ast}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}r_{1}^{*}

\displaystyle-{1\over m}\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg\{\operatorname{tr}\big\{\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D^{*}_{1}(z)^{-1}\big\}\bigg\}\bigg]\bigg\}

\displaystyle=-m\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)^{2}\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg\{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)\Big[{r_{1}^{\ast}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}r_{1}^{*}\Big]\bigg\}

\displaystyle+\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)^{2}\bigg(m\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg\{\beta_{1}^{*}(z)\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)^{2}\bigg[{r_{1}^{\ast}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}r_{1}^{*}

\displaystyle-{1\over m}\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg\{\operatorname{tr}\big\{\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D^{*}_{1}(z)^{-1}\big\}\bigg\}\bigg]\bigg\}\bigg)+o_{\mathbb{P},{\rm unif}}(1)

\displaystyle=-m\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)^{2}\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg\{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)\Big[{r_{1}^{\ast}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}r_{1}^{*}\Big]\bigg\}

(E.80)

\displaystyle+\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)^{2}\bigg(m\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg\{\beta_{1}^{*}(z)\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)^{2}\bigg[{r_{1}^{\ast}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}r_{1}^{*}

(E.81)

\displaystyle-{1\over m}\operatorname{tr}\big\{\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D^{*}_{1}(z)^{-1}\big\}\bigg]\bigg\}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)^{2}{\rm Cov}^{*}\Big(\beta_{1}^{*}(z)\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)^{2},{\tr}\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}\big)\Big)+o_{\mathbb{P},{\rm unif}}(1)

(E.82)

uniformly for
z\in{\cal C}_{n}
,
where we have used Lemma
E.11
and
E.12
. We now prove that the term (
E.82
) is of order
o_{\mathbb{P},{\rm unif}}(1)
.
Note first that by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\Big|\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)^{2}{\rm Cov}^{*}\Big(\beta_{1}^{*}(z)\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)^{2},{\tr}\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}\big)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle~~\leq\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}|\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)|\big\{\mathbb{E}^{*}[|\beta_{1}^{*}(z)|^{2}|\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)|^{4}]\big\}^{1/2}

(E.83)

\displaystyle~~\times\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}|\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)|\big\{\mathbb{E}^{*}\big|{\tr}\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}\big)

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~-\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[{\tr}\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}\big)\big]\big|^{2}\big\}^{1/2}

First, note that by Lemma
E.11
it follows that

\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}|\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)~.

Moreover, it is easy to see that

\mathbb{E}^{*}[|\beta_{1}^{*}(z)|^{2}|\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)|^{4}\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}^{c}}]=o_{\mathbb{P},{\rm unif}}(1)
. On the other hand on
{\cal A}_{n}
it follows from (
E.67
) that

\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}[|\beta_{1}^{*}(z)|^{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)^{4}|\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}]\lesssim\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}[||\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)|^{4}\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}]

Recalling the definition of
\varepsilon_{1}^{*}(z)
in (
A.5
), Burkholder’s inequality, we obtain the bound (for any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
)

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)|^{4}\big]\lesssim\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|\gamma_{1}^{*}(z)-\varepsilon_{1}^{*}(z)|^{4}\big]+\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|\varepsilon^{*}_{1}(z)|^{4}\big]

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\Big|{1\over m}{\rm tr}(\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}^{c}}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big]\Big|^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}{1\over m^{4}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big|\sum_{j=2}^{m}(\mathbb{E}^{*}_{j}-\mathbb{E}^{*}_{j-1})\beta_{1j}^{*}(z){r_{j}^{*}}^{\top}D_{1j}^{*}(z)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D_{1j}^{*}(z)^{-1}{r_{j}^{*}}\Big|^{4}+\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|\varepsilon^{*}_{1}(z)|^{4}\big]

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+O_{\mathbb{P}}\big(m^{4(1+\alpha)-\ell}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim{1\over m^{2}}\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[\big|\beta_{12}^{*}(z){r_{2}^{*}}^{\top}D_{12}^{*}(z)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D_{12}^{*}(z)^{-1}{r_{2}^{*}}\big|^{4}\big]+\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|\varepsilon^{*}_{1}(z)|^{4}\big]

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+O_{\mathbb{P}}\big(m^{4(1+\alpha)-\ell}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim{1\over m^{2}}\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[\big|\beta_{12}^{*}(z){r_{2}^{*}}^{\top}D_{12}^{*}(z)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D_{12}^{*}(z)^{-1}{r_{2}^{*}}\big|^{4}\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}\big]+\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|\varepsilon^{*}_{1}(z)|^{4}\big]

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+O_{\mathbb{P}}\big(m^{4(1+\alpha)-\ell}\big)

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}\Big({1\over m^{2}}\Big)+O_{\mathbb{P}}\big(m^{4(1+\alpha)-\ell}\big)+\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|\varepsilon^{*}_{1}(z)|^{4}\big],

where
\beta_{12}^{*}(z)
is defined in (
A.11
). Note that on the set
{\mathcal{A}_{n}}
,

is bounded (see (

)) and we have

and

)). Moreover, by Proposition
E.3
, we have

\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|\varepsilon^{*}_{1}(z)|^{4}\big]=O_{\mathbb{P}}\Big({\delta_{m}^{4}\over m}+\frac{m}{n}\Big),

which gives

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)|^{4}\big]=O_{\mathbb{P}}\Big({1\over m^{2}}\Big)+O_{\mathbb{P}}\Big({\delta_{m}^{4}\over m}+\frac{m}{n}\Big)

(E.84)

This implies that the first factor in (
E.83
) converges to
0
in probabity. Moreover, we note for later purposes that this also reveals the approximation

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}|\beta_{1}^{*}(z)-\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

(E.85)

We now consider the second factor in (
E.83
) and introduce the notation

M(z)=(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}.

Similar arguments as used in equation (4.7) in
Bai and Silverstein, (2004)
give

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big|{\tr}\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}M(z)\big)-\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[{\tr}\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}M(z)\big)\big]\big|^{2}

(E.86)

\displaystyle~~~~~=\sum_{j=2}^{m}\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big|(\mathbb{E}_{j}^{*}-\mathbb{E}_{j-1}^{*})\big[\beta_{1j}^{*}(z){r_{j}^{*}}^{\top}D_{1j}^{*}(z)^{-1}M(z)D_{1j}^{*}(z)^{-1}{r_{j}^{*}}\big]\big|^{2}

\displaystyle~~~~~\leq 8\sum_{j=2}^{m}\bigg\{\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big|\bar{\beta}_{1j}^{*}(z)\big\{{r_{j}^{*}}^{\top}D_{1j}^{*}(z)^{-1}M(z)D_{1j}^{*}(z)^{-1}{r_{j}^{*}}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~-{1\over m}\tr\big(\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D_{1j}^{*}(z)^{-1}M(z)D_{1j}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big)\}\big|^{2}

\displaystyle~~~~~+\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|\bar{\beta}_{1j}^{*}(z)-\beta_{1j}^{*}(z)|^{2}\big|{r_{j}^{*}}^{\top}D_{1j}^{*}(z)^{-1}M(z)D_{1j}^{*}(z)^{-1}{r_{j}^{*}}\big|^{2}\big]\bigg\},

where

\bar{\beta}_{1j}
is defined in (
A.14
).
Proposition
E.3
gives for the first sum the order
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. For the second sum we note that it sufficient to consider the estimates on
{\cal A}_{n}
. The second factor in this term is stochastically bounded (on
{\cal A}_{n}
) and it remains to consider the first factor. Here we use the identity

\beta_{1j}^{*}(z)-\bar{\beta}_{1j}^{*}(z)=\beta_{1j}^{*}(z)\bar{\beta}_{1j}^{*}(z)\varepsilon_{1j}^{*}(z)
and obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}|\bar{\beta}_{1j}^{*}(z)-\beta_{1j}^{*}(z)|^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}|\varepsilon_{1j}^{*}(z)|^{2}\big]=O_{\mathbb{P}}\Big({1\over m}\Big)

by Proposition
E.3
and (
E.67
). This shows that (
E.86
) is stochastically bounded,
that is

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big|{\tr}\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}M(z)\big)-\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[{\tr}\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}M(z)\big)\big]\big|^{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

(E.87)

This gives for the term (
E.82
)

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}|\eqref{rev20c}|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\Big({\delta_{m}^{4}}+\frac{m^{2}}{n}\Big)^{1/2}\Big)=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Similarly, using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality together with Proposition
E.3
and (
E.84
) we obtain for the term (
E.81
)
and obtain by

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}|\eqref{rev20b}|

\displaystyle\leq m\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}|\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)|^{2}\Big(\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[|\beta_{1}^{*}(z)|^{2}|\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)|^{4}\big]

\displaystyle~~~~~\times\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big[\big|{r_{1}^{\ast}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}r_{1}^{*}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~-{1\over m}\operatorname{tr}\big\{\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D^{*}_{1}(z)^{-1}\big\}\big|^{2}\Big]\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

Finally, we derive a uniform approximation in probability for the term (
E.80
) we introduce the notaion

H(z)=\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}

and
observe the decomposition

\displaystyle m\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big[\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)\big[{r_{1}^{\ast}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}H(z)r_{1}^{*}\big]\Big]

\displaystyle~~~=m\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big[\Big({r_{1}^{\ast}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}r_{1}^{\ast}-{1\over m}\tr\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}\big)\Big)

(E.88)

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\times\Big({r_{1}^{\ast}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}H(z)r_{1}^{\ast}-{1\over m}\tr\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}H(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}\big)\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle~~~+{1\over m}{\rm Cov\big(\tr(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}),\tr(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}H(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n})\big)}

(E.89)

\displaystyle~~~-\tr\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}H(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}\big]\big)\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big\{{r_{1}^{\ast}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}H(z)r_{1}^{\ast}

(E.90)

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~-{1\over m}\tr\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}H(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}\big)\Big\}

\displaystyle~~~+\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big[\tr\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}\big)\Big\{{r_{1}^{\ast}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}H(z)r_{1}^{\ast}-{1\over m}\tr\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}H(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}\big)\Big\}\Big]

(E.91)

\displaystyle~~~+\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big[\tr\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}H(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}\big)\Big\{{r_{1}^{\ast}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}r_{1}^{\ast}-{1\over m}\tr\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}\big)\Big\}\Big]~.

(E.92)

By Lemma
E.12
it follows that the terms (
E.90
) - (
E.92
) are of order
o_{\mathbb{P},{\rm unif}}(1)
.
The Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the same arguments as given in the derivation of (
E.87
) show that the term (
E.89
) is of order

o_{\mathbb{P},{\rm unif}}(1)

as well, which gives

\displaystyle m\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big[\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z){r_{1}^{\ast}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}H(z)r_{1}^{*}\Big]

\displaystyle~~~=m\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big[\Big({r_{1}^{\ast}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}r_{1}^{\ast}-{1\over m}\tr\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}\big)\Big)

(E.93)

\displaystyle~~~~~\times\Big({r_{1}^{\ast}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}H(z)r_{1}^{\ast}-{1\over m}\tr\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}H(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}\big)\Big)\Big]+o_{\mathbb{P},{\rm unif}}(1).

Using (
E.85
)
and this estimate in (
E.80
) yields for (
E.79
)

\displaystyle mA_{n}^{*}(z)=

\displaystyle m\Big({q\over m}\int{d\mu^{\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}}(t)\over 1+\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}^{*}_{n}(z)}+z{q\over m}\mathbb{E}^{*}{m}^{*}_{n}(z)\Big)

\displaystyle=\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)^{2}\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[{r_{1}^{*}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}r_{1}^{*}\big]

(E.94)

\displaystyle-\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)^{2}m\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big[\Big({r_{1}^{\ast}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}r_{1}^{\ast}-{1\over m}\tr\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle~~~~~\times\Big({r_{1}^{\ast}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}H(z)r_{1}^{\ast}-{1\over m}\tr\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}H(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}\big)\Big)\Big]+o_{\mathbb{P},{\rm unif}}(1)

\displaystyle=S_{n}(z)+o_{\mathbb{P},{\rm unif}}(1),

(E.95)

It now follows from Lemma
E.11
and Proposition
E.3
that the sequence
(S_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is uniformly bounded in probability. Moreover, similar arguments show that the sequence of derivatives on
{\cal C}_{n}
is uniformly bounded in probability as well.
Therefore, the sequence
(\tilde{S}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is equicontinuous on

{\cal C}\cap\mathbb{C}^{+}
, where
\tilde{S}_{n}
is the constant continuation of
S_{n}
from
{\cal C}_{n}
to

{\cal C}\cap\mathbb{C}^{+}
.
Consequently, the uniform limit of
(mA_{n}^{*}(z))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

can be determined pointwise (by the Arzelà-Ascoli Theorem).

This limit can now be obtained by exactly the same arguments as given on page
589
-
592
in
Bai and Silverstein, (2004)
, where the expectation is replaced by the conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}^{*}
and the convergence is correspondingly in probability. This gives

mA_{n}^{*}(z)\to-\int\frac{(\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0}(z))^{2}t^{2}\,d{H}(t)}{(1+t\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0}(z))^{3}}\Big[1-c\int\frac{(\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0}(z))^{2}t^{2}\,d{H}(t)}{(1+t\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0}(z))^{2}}\Big]^{-1}

Combining this limit with the limit in (
E.76
) and observing (
E.74
) finally gives

\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\bigg|\mathbb{E}\big[\widehat{M}_{n}^{*}(z)\big]-c\int\frac{(\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0}(z))^{3}t^{2}\,d{H}(t)}{(1+t\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0}(z))^{3}}\Big[1-c\int\frac{(\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0}(z))^{2}t^{2}\,d{H}(t)}{(1+t\underline{m}_{c,H}^{0}(z))^{2}}\Big]^{-2}\bigg|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

∎

E.4.1
Auxiliary results for the proof of Proposition
D.4

Lemma E.9
.

\sup_{z\in\mathcal{C}_{n}}\big|\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)-\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z)\big|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

The proof follows by
the same arguments as given in the derivation of equation (4.1) in
Bai and Silverstein, (2004)

(see also equation (
B.29
)), noting that

\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[{\mu}^{\Sigma_{n}^{*}}\big]\Rightarrow\mu_{H}^{0}
in probability by dominated convergence convergence.
∎

Lemma E.10
.

\sup_{z\in\mathcal{C}_{n}}\|(\ \mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I)^{-1}\big\|_{S_{\infty}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

A simple calculation shows

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in\mathcal{C}_{n}}\|(\ \mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I)^{-1}\big\|_{S_{\infty}}\leq\Big[\inf_{z\in\mathcal{C}_{n}}\min_{\|x\|_{2}=1}\big\|\big(\underline{m}_{0}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I)\big)x\big\|_{2}-R_{n}\Big]^{-1},

where

R_{n}=\sup_{z\in\mathcal{C}_{n}}\big|\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)-\underline{\tilde{m}}_{n}^{0}(z)\big|\cdot\big\|\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}\big\|_{S_{\infty}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

where the last estimate follows by Lemma
E.9
.
∎

Lemma E.11
.

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}|\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}(z)|

\displaystyle=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

(E.96)

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}|\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

(E.97)

Note that
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{*}
depends on
n
and in this proof we highlight this dependence by the notation
\tilde{\gamma}_{1,n}^{*}
. Let be
an open connected and bounded set
D
such that
{\cal C}\cap\mathbb{C}^{+}\subset D\subset\mathbb{C}^{+}
and
\bar{D}\cap[K_{\rm left},K_{\rm right}]=\emptyset
.
Recalling the definition of the set
{\cal A}_{n}
in (
E.9
) we have

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in D}\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}\big|{r_{1}^{*}}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}r_{1}^{*}\big|\leq\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}\|{r_{1}^{*}}\|^{2}\sup_{z\in D}\|D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\|_{{\cal S}_{\infty}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in D}\Big|{1\over m}{\rm tr}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\big[\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}L_{n}L_{n}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big]\big)\Big|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

Consequently,

\sup_{z\in D}|\tilde{\gamma}_{1,n}^{*}(z)|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Therefore, for any
\delta>0
, there exists a constant
K_{\delta}>0
such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\big(B_{n,\delta}\big)\leq\delta~,

(E.98)

where

\displaystyle B_{n,\delta}=\Big\{\omega\in\Omega~\Big|~\sup_{z\in D}|\tilde{\gamma}_{1,n}^{*}(z)|>K_{\delta}\Big\}

(E.99)

As shown in (
E.84
), we have for each
z\in D

\displaystyle\tilde{\gamma}_{1,n}^{*}(z)=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(E.100)

Let
z_{1},z_{2},\ldots
denote a subsequence in
D
with accumulation point in
D
and let
n_{k}
be an arbitrary subsequence. Then there exists a further subsequence
n_{k}^{\prime}
such that

\tilde{\gamma}_{1,n_{k}^{\prime}}^{*}(z_{1})=o(1)

on a set
N_{1}^{c}\subset\Omega
with
\mathbb{P}(N_{1})=0
. By the same reasoning there exists a further subsequencene
{n_{k}^{\prime\prime}}
of
{n_{k}^{\prime}}
such that

\tilde{\gamma}_{1,n_{k}^{\prime\prime}}^{*}(z_{2})=o(1)

on a set
N_{2}^{c}\subset\Omega
with
\mathbb{P}(N_{2})=0
. By Cantor’s diagonalization principle we obtain a sequence denoted by
\tilde{n}_{k}
such that

\tilde{\gamma}_{1,\tilde{n}_{k}}^{*}(z_{j})=o(1)

for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
and all
\omega
outside the null set
N=\cup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}N_{j}\subset\Omega
. Moreover, the sequence of functions
z\to\tilde{\gamma}^{*}_{1,\tilde{n}_{k}}(z)(\omega)\mathds{1}_{B_{\tilde{n}_{k},\delta}^{c}}(\omega)
is bounded for every
\omega\in N^{c}
. By Vitali’s Theorem the sequence
z\to\tilde{\gamma}^{*}_{1,\tilde{n}_{k}}(z)(\omega)\mathds{1}_{B_{\tilde{n}_{k},\delta}^{c}}(\omega)
converges uniformly to
0
on
D
for every
\omega\in N^{c}
. As the initial sequence
n_{k}
was arbitrary we conclude

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(\sup_{z\in D}\big|\tilde{\gamma}^{*}_{1,n}(z)\mathds{1}_{B_{n,\delta}^{c}}\big|>\eta\Big)=0

for any
\eta>0
. Finally, we obtain from the definition of
B_{n,\delta}

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(\sup_{z\in D}\big|\tilde{\gamma}^{*}_{1,n}(z)\big|>\eta\Big)\leq\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(\sup_{z\in D}\big|\tilde{\gamma}^{*}_{1,n}(z)\mathds{1}_{B_{n,\delta}}\big|>{\eta\over 2}\Big)\leq\delta~.

As
\delta>0
was arbitrary the assertion (
E.96
) follows.

To prove (
E.97
) we note that the statement is obvious for the functions
z\to\tilde{b}_{n}^{*}(z)\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}^{c}}
. On the other hand we obtain on
{\cal A}_{n}
from (
E.58
)

b_{n}^{*}(z)={\tilde{\beta}_{1}^{*}(z)\over 1-\beta_{1}^{*}(z)\tilde{\gamma}_{1,n}^{*}(z)}~.

By (
E.67
)
\beta_{1}^{*}
is uniformly bounded on
{\cal A}_{n}
and the assertion follows from (
E.96
).
∎

Lemma E.12
.

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{*}\Big|\mathbb{E}^{*}_{X_{1}^{*}}\Big[{X_{1}^{\ast}}^{\top}C^{*}(z)X_{1}^{*}-\operatorname{tr}\big\{C^{*}(z)\big\}\Big]\Big|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

(E.101)

where, for example

\displaystyle M(z)

\displaystyle=L_{n}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}L_{n}

\displaystyle M(z)

\displaystyle={\tr\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}\big)\over m}L_{n}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}L_{n}^{\top}

\displaystyle M(z)

\displaystyle={\tr\big(D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}\big)\over m}L_{n}^{\top}D_{1}^{*}(z)^{-1}\big(\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}_{n}^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}+I_{q}\big)^{-1}L_{n}

Let
\hat{I}=\{i_{1}^{*},\ldots,i_{m}^{*}\}
denote the random subset of chosen indices by the bootstrap and note that
\#\hat{I}\leq m
, then

\displaystyle m\mathbb{E}^{*}_{X_{1}^{*}}\Big[{r_{1}^{\ast}}^{\top}C^{*}(z)r_{1}^{*}-\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{E}^{*}\operatorname{tr}\big\{C^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}\big\}\Big]={1\over n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}^{\top}C^{*}(z)X_{i}-\mathbb{E}^{*}\operatorname{tr}\big\{C^{*}(z)\tilde{\Sigma}_{n}\big\}

\displaystyle={1\over n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}}\Big\{\sum_{k=1}^{q^{\prime}}(X_{ik}^{2}-1)C_{kk}^{*}(z)+\sum_{k\neq k^{\prime}}^{q^{\prime}}X_{ik}X_{ik^{\prime}}C_{kk^{\prime}}^{*}(z)\Big\}

(E.102)

\displaystyle+{1\over n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}^{c}}\Big\{\sum_{k=1}^{q^{\prime}}(X_{ik}^{2}-1)C_{kk}^{*}(z)+\sum_{k\neq k^{\prime}}^{q^{\prime}}X_{ik}X_{ik^{\prime}}C_{kk^{\prime}}^{*}(z)\Big\}

We now investigate both terms separately:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\Big|{1\over n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}}\Big\{\sum_{k=1}^{q^{\prime}}(X_{ik}^{2}-1)C_{kk}^{*}(z)+\sum_{k\neq k^{\prime}}^{q^{\prime}}X_{ik}X_{ik^{\prime}}C_{kk^{\prime}}^{*}(z)\Big\}\Big|\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\Big|{1\over n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}}\Big\{\sum_{k=1}^{q^{\prime}}(X_{ik}^{2}-1)C_{kk}^{*}(z)+\sum_{k\neq k^{\prime}}^{q^{\prime}}X_{ik}X_{ik^{\prime}}C_{kk^{\prime}}^{*}(z)\Big\}\Big|~\big|\hat{I}\Big]\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\Big|{1\over n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}}\Big\{\sum_{k=1}^{q^{\prime}}(X_{ik}^{2}-1)C_{kk}^{*}(z)\Big|+\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\Big|{1\over n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}}\sum_{k\neq k^{\prime}}^{q^{\prime}}X_{ik}X_{ik^{\prime}}C_{kk^{\prime}}^{*}(z)\Big\}\Big|~\big|\hat{I}\Big]\Big]

(E.103)

For the first term we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{q^{\prime}}{1\over n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}}(X_{ik}^{2}-1)C_{kk}^{*}(z)\Big|~\big|~\hat{I}\Big]\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big\{\sum_{k=1}^{q^{\prime}}\Big({1\over n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}}(X_{ik}^{2}-1)\Big)^{2}\Big\}^{1/2}\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\Big\{\sum_{k=1}^{q^{\prime}}|C_{kk}^{*}(z)|^{2}\Big\}^{1/2}\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}\Big|~~\hat{I}\Big]\Big]+o(1)

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{m}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{q^{\prime}}\Big({1\over n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}}(X_{ik}^{2}-1)\Big)^{2}\Big\}^{1/2}\leq C{m^{3/2}\over n}+o(1)=o(1)

(E.104)

For the second term we obtain similarly

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\Big|{1\over n}\sum_{k\neq k^{\prime}}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}}^{q^{\prime}}X_{ik}X_{ik^{\prime}}C_{kk^{\prime}}^{*}(z)\Big\}\Big|~\big|\hat{I}\Big]\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big\{\sum_{k\neq k^{\prime}}\Big({1\over n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}}^{q^{\prime}}X_{ik}X_{ik^{\prime}}\Big)^{2}\Big\}^{1/2}\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\Big\{\sum_{k\neq k^{\prime}}|C_{kk^{\prime}}^{*}(z)|^{2}\Big\}^{1/2}\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}~\Big|\hat{I}\Big]\Big]+o(1)

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\sqrt{m}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{k\neq k^{\prime}}{1\over n^{2}}\sum_{i,j\in\hat{I}}X_{ik}X_{ik^{\prime}}X_{jk}X_{jk^{\prime}}\Big]\Big\}^{1/2}~\Big|~\hat{I}\Big]\Big]+o(1)

\displaystyle\quad\leq C{m^{2}\over n}+o(1)=o(1)~,

which proves that the first term in (
E.102
) is of order
o(1)
. To derive a corresponding statement for the second term, we note that similar aguments as given in (
E.104
) show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{q^{\prime}}{1\over n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}^{c}}(X_{ik}^{2}-1)C_{kk}^{*}(z)\Big|~\big|~\hat{I}\Big]\Big]\leq C{m\over\sqrt{n}}+o(1)=o(1)

(E.105)

For the remaining term we use a chaining argument. For this purpose we introduce the notation
\Delta_{kk^{\prime}}(z_{1},z_{2})=C_{kk^{\prime}}(z_{1})-C_{kk^{\prime}}(z_{2})
, defined
\Delta(z_{1},z_{2})
as the corresponding matrix with these entries and note that conditional on
\hat{I}
the random variables

\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}
and

\Delta_{kk^{\prime}}(z_{1},z_{2})
is independent of
X_{i}
, whenever
i\in\hat{I}^{c}
. This yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}\Big|{1\over n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}^{c}}\sum_{k\neq k^{\prime}}X_{ik}X_{ik^{\prime}}\Delta_{kk^{\prime}}(z_{1},z_{2})\Big|^{2}~\Big|~\hat{I}\Big]\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad={1\over n^{2}}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}^{c}}\sum_{k_{1}\neq k_{1}^{\prime}}\sum_{k_{2}\neq k_{2}^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\Big\{\mathbb{E}[X_{ik_{1}}X_{ik_{1}^{\prime}}X_{ik_{2}}X_{ik_{2}^{\prime}}~|~\hat{I}]\cdot\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}\Delta_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}(z_{1},z_{2})\bar{\Delta}_{k_{2}k_{1}^{\prime}}(z_{1},z_{2})~|~\hat{I}]\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad\leq{2\over n}\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbb{E}\big[\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}\|\Delta(z_{1},z_{2})\|^{2}_{S_{2}}|\hat{I}\big]\big\}\leq 2{m\over n}|z_{1}-z_{2}|^{2}

Now note that we have

\displaystyle\Delta(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=C^{*}(z_{1})-C^{*}(z_{2})

\displaystyle=(M(z_{1})-M(z_{2}))D_{1}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}+M(z_{2})(D_{1}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}-D_{1}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1})

Therefore we obtain

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}\|\Delta(z_{1},z_{2})\|_{S_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{m}\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}\|\Delta(z_{1},z_{2})\|_{S_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{m}\big\{\|(M(z_{1})-M(z_{2})\|_{S_{\infty}}+\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}\|D_{1}^{*}(z_{1})^{-1}-D_{1}^{*}(z_{2})^{-1}\|_{S_{\infty}}\big\}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{m}C|z_{1}-z_{2}|,

where we have used the fact that the function
z\to\mathbb{E}^{*}\underline{m}^{*}(z)
is analytic, (4.3) c.f. in
Bai and Silverstein, (2004)
. This finally yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}\Big|{1\over n}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}^{c}}\sum_{k\neq k^{\prime}}X_{ik}X_{ik^{\prime}}\Delta_{kk^{\prime}}(z_{1},z_{2})\Big|^{2}\Big]\leq C{m\over n}|z_{1}-z_{2}|^{2}~.

Employing standard chaining with the Young-Orlicz module
\phi(t)=t^{2}
reveals the chaining bound

\int_{0}^{\ell\sqrt{m\over n}}\sqrt{\sqrt{m}\over\varepsilon\sqrt{n}}d\leq C\sqrt{m\over n}

where
\ell
denotes the length of the curve
{\cal C}
. Hence, it follows that

\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}\sup_{z\in{\cal C}_{n}}\Big|{1\over n}\sum_{k\neq k^{\prime}}\sum_{i\in\hat{I}^{c}}^{q^{\prime}}X_{ik}X_{ik^{\prime}}C_{kk^{\prime}}^{*}(z)\Big\}\Big]\Big]=O\Big(\sqrt{m\over n}\Big).

The term corresponding to

\mathds{1}_{{\cal A}_{n}}
can be treated by the same arguments as given for the second term in the last line of (
E.103
), which completes the proof.
∎
